Instructions
Scientific tasks protocol v2 — human-in-the-loop scientific workflow.

1. Formulate an appropriate mathematical model yourself. Separate facts supplied by the user from choices or extra assumptions you introduce. Prefer deriving or estimating model parameters from the supplied observations and, when useful, requested experiments. State and justify the model family and observation model needed for that inference. For example, assume and justify a distribution family explicitly, then derive its parameters from the supplied moments, observations or experiment results; do not assume both the family and a complete set of parameter values when those values are what the task requires you to infer. Do not choose convenient parameter values and merely show that they reproduce the observations, presenting that exercise as inference. If the observations do not determine the parameters, expose what remains undetermined and what additional information or assumptions would be needed. Do not silently turn population moments into a fully specified prior. Do not assume the conclusion you are asked to establish as a premise.
2. If the information does not determine a unique answer, explain why and give a conditional answer with its assumptions exposed. You may revise the model and conclusion across attempts; preserve the reasons and lineage.
3. Write a substantive Lean proof covering the scientific inference needed to answer the question, connecting the supplied information and explicit modeling choices to the claimed result. Use Bayesian inference where appropriate; it is not mandatory for every task. A proof of arithmetic for a number calculated elsewhere is insufficient. The human will assess scientific relevance and whether the proof covers the actual inference.
4. Use available tools, including Internet research. Request literature, calculations, experiments or clarification from the operator when useful; a request is optional. Use the request tool below, stop for a real response, and incorporate the response with its source and limitations. Never invent an external response.
5. Run the actual check tool. Read and fix its real errors, retrying without replacing science with a smaller unrelated theorem. Preserve model changes and raw traces.
6. You may introduce additional premises or axioms, but expose them for review. Any sorry or additional axiom is conditional, never an unconditional full pass. Lean correctness does not establish the truth of the premises.
7. Extract definitions, constants, data, assumptions and the exact conclusions. The harness independently compiles and compares the extracted statements and runs a separate kernel check. A failed or unknown check is not success.
8. A human reviews the actual content and may accept or reject it with feedback. If the researcher rejects the assumptions or conclusions, revise your model, assumptions, conclusions and proof in response to the feedback. Never accept your own result or claim human approval.

Human-readability requirements:
- Write the formalization so a human scientist can understand what is assumed and what is concluded, with a concise plain-language translation alongside the relevant Lean statements. Explain the real-world meaning of the model and the reason for each scientific modeling choice.
- Keep the assumptions, scientifically meaningful definitions and final conclusions as small and direct as possible. Avoid redundant definitions, duplicate specializations of the same formula, synonyms, and layers of wrappers that merely rename an expression. Retain a helper definition only when it materially improves clarity or is mathematically necessary.
- Put derivable facts and technical work into intermediate lemmas or proof-local helpers: algebra, normalization, parameter calculations and other consequences should be proved rather than added to the review assumptions. Keep the final conclusion focused on the actual scientific answer.
- Minimizing the review boundary must preserve its meaning: expose every independent modeling choice and every definition needed to interpret the conclusion. Do not hide assumptions in intermediate lemmas, use unproved facts as lemmas, or shorten the conclusion into an unexplained proposition alias.
- Before submission, simplify the public statement and its dependency chain, and give a compact review section separating supplied information, additional assumptions, necessary definitions and proved conclusions. Definitions introduced solely for proof organization should stay inside the proof when possible. Do not optimize for a short file at the cost of a longer or less intelligible assumptions/conclusions packet.

Isolation rule: work only from this task, these instructions, native installed libraries, and the run's own history and supplied responses. Do not seek previous solutions, task-specific proofs, or preset model families, parameter values or expected answers from earlier runs. You may consult general scientific or statistical literature to select and justify a model family. Authentication metadata is mounted only because the native subscription client needs its existing login; it is not scientific evidence. Do not read, print, copy or modify authentication files. Never use paid APIs, API-key fallback, other agents, or external services requiring payment. Stop on subscription quota/authentication errors.

Tools (run in the terminal):
  python /harness/src/vcs_protocol.py tool check --manifest /work/candidate.json
  python /harness/src/vcs_protocol.py tool request --kind literature --question '...'
Kinds are literature, calculation, experiment, clarification. A request stops this cycle; the operator supplies an answer and resumes. No physical measurement device is connected. You may request a real measurement; do not report an unperformed measurement. Any synthetic calculation or simulated experiment must be explicitly labeled as such and is evidence about its chosen model, not a measurement of the physical system. If no measurement is available, provide a scientifically useful conditional prediction or range with the missing information and assumptions stated, rather than claiming an unconditional numerical answer. Record incorporated response IDs in the candidate. Evidence is user/external supplied and not guaranteed true.

Write /work/candidate.json with this shape (all paths relative to /work):
{"proof_file":"Model.lean", "narrative_file":"report.txt", "conclusions":{"answer":"answer_theorem"}, "user_facts":["..."], "model_choices":["..."], "sources":["..."], "incorporated_inputs":[], "proof_scope":"What the theorems actually establish and what is left to human review"}
List only the final scientific conclusion or conclusions needed to answer the task, with nonempty labels mapped to actual, distinct Lean theorem declarations. A single answer theorem is allowed. Supporting model, inference, normalization and other derived facts may be intermediate lemmas; there are no mandatory model/update/prediction conclusion roles. Use plain top-level `theorem name : statement := proof` declarations, with all binders inside `statement`. Imports must be explicit `import Mathlib.…` lines at the start of the file; imports of project/task files are unavailable. The restricted proof language supports definitions, structures, lemmas, axioms and sorry but no executable metaprogramming, custom syntax, namespaces, native_decide, IO or unsafe code. Check feedback gives concrete unsupported syntax errors. Native Mathlib tactics are available. Explain any restriction that prevents a scientifically meaningful formalization; do not pretend a narrower result meets the task.
Task
I want to boil saline water. I have poured 1 litre of water at 20 degrees Celsius into a pot and started heating it on a stove. I will add salt, and want the final salted water to reach its boiling point as quickly as possible. The salt quantity, pot, heater and room conditions have not otherwise been specified. At what water temperature, or after how much heating time, should I add the salt to make the salted water boil sooner?

{
  "command": "cd /work; cat > Model.lean <<'EOF'\nimport Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Integrals\nimport Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Integral.IntervalIntegral.Basic\n\n/-! Model: enthalpy coordinate H (J, measured from the state \"everything at room temperature Tr\").\n Pure water (heat capacity Cw) has temperature Tr + H/Cw.\n Salted water (heat capacity Cw+Cs, constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D ≥ 0) has\n temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is stirred in instantly: H is conserved.\n Heater delivers constant power P, losses are Newtonian h*(T-Tr), so dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr)\n and the time to go from H1 to H2 is ∫ dH / (P - h*(T(H)-Tr)).\n Tb = boiling point of the FINAL salt solution: fixed by the salt quantity, not by timing. -/\n\n/-- Total time to reach Tb when the salt is added once the water has reached temperature Ta. -/\nnoncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=\n  (∫ H in (Cw*(T0-Tr))..(Cw*(Ta-Tr)), 1/(P - h*(H/Cw))) +\n  ∫ H in (Cw*(Ta-Tr))..((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D), 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))\n\nlemma boilTime_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ)\n    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hCs : 0 ≤ Cs) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hh : 0 ≤ h)\n    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P) (hTr : Tr ≤ T0) (h1 : T0 ≤ Ta1) (h12 : Ta1 ≤ Ta2) (h2 : Ta2 ≤ Tb) :\n    boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 := by\n  have hC : 0 < Cw + Cs := by linarith\n  set c := Cw*(T0-Tr) with hc\n  set a := Cw*(Ta1-Tr) with ha\n  set b := Cw*(Ta2-Tr) with hb\n  set e := (Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D with he\n  have hca : c ≤ a := by rw [hc, ha]; nlinarith\n  have hab : a ≤ b := by rw [ha, hb]; nlinarith\n  have hbe : b ≤ e := by\n    rw [hb, he]; nlinarith [mul_nonneg hCs (show 0 ≤ Tb - Tr by linarith)]\n  have ha0 : 0 ≤ a := by rw [ha]; nlinarith\n  -- positivity of denominators\n  have hpw : ∀ H, H ≤ b → 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by\n    intro H hH\n    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by\n      rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; rw [hb] at hH; nlinarith\n    nlinarith\n  have hps : ∀ H, H ≤ e → 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) := by\n    intro H hH\n    have : (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Tb - Tr := by\n      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; rw [he] at hH; nlinarith\n    nlinarith\n  have cw : ContinuousOn (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*(H/Cw))) (Set.Icc c b) := by\n    apply ContinuousOn.div continuousOn_const\n    · fun_prop\n    · intro H hH; exact (hpw H hH.2).ne'\n  have cs : ContinuousOn (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) (Set.Icc a e) := by\n    apply ContinuousOn.div continuousOn_const\n    · fun_prop\n    · intro H hH; exact (hps H hH.2).ne'\n  have iw : ∀ x y, c ≤ x → x ≤ y → y ≤ b →\n      IntervalIntegrable (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*(H/Cw))) MeasureTheory.volume x y := by\n    intro x y hx hxy hy\n    apply ContinuousOn.intervalIntegrable\n    rw [Set.uIcc_of_le hxy]\n    exact cw.mono (Set.Icc_subset_Icc hx hy)\n  have is : ∀ x y, a ≤ x → x ≤ y → y ≤ e →\n      IntervalIntegrable (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) MeasureTheory.volume x y := by\n    intro x y hx hxy hy\n    apply ContinuousOn.intervalIntegrable\n    rw [Set.uIcc_of_le hxy]\n    exact cs.mono (Set.Icc_subset_Icc hx hy)\n  unfold boilTime\n  have s1 := intervalIntegral.integral_add_adjacent_intervals\n    (iw c a hc.le hca (hab.trans le_rfl)) (iw a b hca hab le_rfl)\n  have s2 := intervalIntegral.integral_add_adjacent_intervals\n    (is a b le_rfl hab hbe) (is b e hab le_rfl le_rfl)\n  have key : (∫ H in a..b, 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) ≤ ∫ H in a..b, 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) := by\n    apply intervalIntegral.integral_mono_on hab (is a b le_rfl hab hbe) (iw a b hca hab le_rfl)\n    intro H hH\n    have hpos := hpw H hH.2\n    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le hpos\n    have : (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw := by\n      rw [div_le_div_iff₀ hC hCw]\n      nlinarith [mul_nonneg hCs (show 0 ≤ H by linarith [hH.1]), mul_nonneg hCw.le hD]\n    nlinarith\n  rw [← s1, ← s2]\n  linarith\n\n/-- ANSWER.  Under the model, the time to boil never decreases if the salt is added later\n(at a hotter water temperature Ta ∈ [T0, Tb]); hence adding it at the start (Ta = T0 = 20 °C)\nis optimal. -/\ntheorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),\n    0 < Cw → 0 ≤ Cs → 0 ≤ D → 0 ≤ h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 → T0 ≤ Tb →\n    (∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →\n        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧\n    (∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →\n        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta) := by\n  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr hT\n  refine ⟨fun a b h1 h12 h2 => boilTime_mono _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr h1 h12 h2, ?_⟩\n  intro Ta hTa hTb\n  exact boilTime_mono _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr le_rfl hTa hTb\nEOF\ncat > report.txt <<'EOF'\nSee below (written after check).\nEOF\ncat > candidate.json <<'EOF'\n{\"proof_file\":\"Model.lean\",\"narrative_file\":\"report.txt\",\"conclusions\":{\"answer\":\"answer_theorem\"},\"user_facts\":[\"1 L of water at 20 C in a pot on a stove\",\"salt will be added; goal: salted water reaches its boiling point as soon as possible\",\"salt quantity, pot, heater, room unspecified\"],\"model_choices\":[\"constant heater power P; Newtonian loss h(T-Tr); lumped heat capacities\",\"salt added at room temperature, instantly mixed, enthalpy conserved\",\"salt raises heat capacity by Cs>=0, constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D>=0\",\"final boiling point Tb depends on salt amount only, not on timing; P>h(Tb-Tr) so boiling is reachable\",\"room temperature Tr<=20 C; evaporation and boil-over ignored\"],\"sources\":[],\"incorporated_inputs\":[],\"proof_scope\":\"Proves, in the stated lumped model, that time to boil is monotone nondecreasing in the water temperature at which salt is added, so adding at the start is optimal. Does not prove strictness, nor magnitude of the gain; model premises are for human review.\"}\nEOF\npython /harness/src/vcs_protocol.py tool check --manifest /work/candidate.json 2>&1 | tail -40"

  "command": "cd /work; cat > Model.lean <<'EOF'\nimport Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv\nimport Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue\n\n/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from \"everything at room temperature Tr\".\n Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.\n Salt solution, heat capacity Cw+Cs, constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D ≥ 0:\n temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.\n Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).\n Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where\n Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).\n Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/\n\n/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/\nnoncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))\n/-- same for the salt solution -/\nnoncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))\n\n/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:\n heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/\nnoncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=\n  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +\n  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))\n\nlemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)\n    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hCs : 0 ≤ Cs) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hh : 0 < h)\n    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P) :\n    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) (Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) := by\n  have hC : 0 < Cw + Cs := by linarith\n  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by\n    intro H hH\n    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; {
  "command": "cd /work; cat > report.txt <<'EOF'\nANSWER (conditional): add the salt at the start (water at 20 C, t = 0). In the model below the\ntime to boil never decreases the later the salt goes in, so no heating delay helps. The gain is\nsmall: the salt's effect on boiling time comes through heat losses only.\n\nWHY (physics). Boiling point of the final solution Tb = 100 C + elevation (colligative; set by salt\namount alone). Energy needed to reach it from the start is fixed whatever the timing. Timing\nmatters only through heat loss: the stove delivers P, the pot loses h(T-Tr). Salt raises the heat\ncapacity, so for the same stored energy the pot is cooler, loses less, and heats faster. Hence\nsalt in early = less loss = earlier boil. If losses were zero (h=0) timing would not matter.\nCommon belief \"salt later because salt makes water heat slower\" is wrong about total time:\nthe energy must be supplied anyway. Adding salt to near-boiling water also risks boil-over (safety, not time).\n\nFACTS SUPPLIED: 1 L water, 20 C, stove heating, salt to be added, aim = earliest boil of salted water;\nsalt amount, pot, heater, room unspecified.\n\nMODEL CHOICES (my assumptions, for review):\n 1 Lumped system, enthalpy coordinate H from \"all at room temperature Tr\"; water T = Tr+H/Cw (Cw includes pot).\n 2 Constant heater power P; Newtonian loss h(T-Tr), h>0; evaporation ignored.\n 3 Salt at Tr, mixed instantly, dissolves instantly; H conserved at mixing.\n 4 Salt solution heat capacity Cw+Cs, Cs>=0; constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D>=0 (NaCl ~ +3.9 kJ/mol).\n 5 Tb independent of timing; P > h(Tb-Tr) (boiling reachable); Tr <= 20 C; salt added before boiling (Ta in [T0,Tb]).\n 6 Time from H1 to H2 = integral dH/(P - h(T(H)-Tr)); closed form via log antiderivatives Fw, Fs\n   (Mathlib in this harness lacks interval integrals); derivative checked in Lean.\nNot determined by the data: salt amount, P, h, Cw, Cs, D, Tr. The conclusion holds for all values satisfying the\nassumptions, so it does not need them; the MAGNITUDE of the gain does and is not claimed (no calculation or measurement run).\n\nLEAN (Model.lean): boilTime = total time as a function of Ta (water temperature when salt is added).\nG_mono proves via derivative (1/dw - 1/ds >= 0, using the proved inequality (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) <= H/Cw for H>=0)\nthat Fw-Fs is monotone. answer_theorem: boilTime is monotone in Ta on [T0,Tb] and boilTime(T0) is the minimum.\nCheck passed, axioms only propext/Classical.choice/Quot.sound, no sorry. assistant
**Answer:** Add the salt at the start, with the water still at 20 °C. In my model, adding it later never makes the salted water boil sooner. Waiting for a hotter temperature gains nothing. The Lean check passed and the candidate is ready for your review.

**Why:**
- The final boiling point, 100 °C plus the salt's elevation, depends only on how much salt you add. The energy needed to reach it is the same whenever the salt goes in.
- Timing only matters through heat loss to the room. Salt raises the heat capacity, so at the same stored energy the pot is cooler and loses less heat.
- Salt added early therefore means less loss and an earlier boil. With zero heat loss, timing would make no difference.
- The gain is probably small. I did not estimate it, and the proof does not give it.
- Adding salt to nearly boiling water can make it boil over. That is a safety point, not a timing one.

**Model assumptions (mine, for you to review):**
- The pot and water are one lump, with constant heater power and heat loss proportional to the excess over room temperature.
- Evaporation is ignored.
- The salt starts at room temperature, dissolves instantly, and its dissolution enthalpy is constant and endothermic (≥ 0).
- The boiling point of the final solution does not depend on when the salt goes in.
- The heater can reach boiling, and the room is at or below 20 °C.
- The salt is added before boiling begins.

**What the proof covers:**
- `answer_theorem` in `Model.lean` proves that the time to boil never decreases as the water temperature at salting rises. It also proves that salting at the start is the minimum.
- The proof assumes nonzero heat loss and rests on a derivative argument, with the time written in closed form using logarithms. This Mathlib build has no interval integrals.
- The axioms are only the standard ones, and there is no `sorry`.
- It does not prove that the gain is strictly positive or how large it is. Whether the premises are realistic is for you to judge.

I made no literature, calculation or experiment requests and ran no measurements or simulations. The salt amount, heater power, heat loss and room temperature were not given. The conclusion holds for any values that meet the assumptions, so it doesn't depend on them.

Files are in `/work`:
- `Model.lean`
- `report.txt`
- `candidate.json`

Produced code

attempt-001/Model.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, heat capacity Cw+Cs, constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D ≥ 0:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hCs : 0 ≤ Cs) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) (Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) := by
  have hC : 0 < Cw + Cs := by linarith
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hle : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw := by
    intro H hH
    rw [div_le_div_iff₀ hC hCw]
    nlinarith [mul_nonneg hCs hH.1, mul_nonneg hCw.le hD]
  have hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) := by
    intro H hH
    have := hle H hH
    have := hw H hH
    nlinarith
  have hd : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
    intro H hH
    have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
      have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
      simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
    have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
      have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
      simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
    have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
    have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
    have := l1.sub l2
    convert this using 1
    · funext x; rfl
    · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
      have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
      have e3 := hh.ne'
      have e4 := hCw.ne'
      have e5 := hC.ne'
      field_simp
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg (convex_Icc _ _)
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H hH).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (interior_subset hH)).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H hH').deriv]
    have := hle H hH'
    have p1 := hw H hH'
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le p1
    nlinarith

/-- ANSWER.  In the model, with salt of fixed amount (Cs, D, Tb fixed), the time to boil
never decreases if the salt is added later, i.e. at a hotter water temperature Ta ∈ [T0, Tb];
hence adding it at the very start (Ta = T0 = 20 °C) is optimal. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 ≤ Cs → 0 ≤ D → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
    (∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr
  have mono : ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 := by
    intro Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
    have G := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hCs hD hh hP
    have m := G (a := Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (b := Cw*(Ta2-Tr))
      ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]
    simp only at m
    linarith
  exact ⟨mono, fun Ta h1 h2 => mono T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2⟩

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Integrals
import Mathlib.MeasureTheory.Integral.IntervalIntegral.Basic

/-! Model: enthalpy coordinate H (J, measured from the state "everything at room temperature Tr").
 Pure water (heat capacity Cw) has temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salted water (heat capacity Cw+Cs, constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D ≥ 0) has
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is stirred in instantly: H is conserved.
 Heater delivers constant power P, losses are Newtonian h*(T-Tr), so dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr)
 and the time to go from H1 to H2 is ∫ dH / (P - h*(T(H)-Tr)).
 Tb = boiling point of the FINAL salt solution: fixed by the salt quantity, not by timing. -/

/-- Total time to reach Tb when the salt is added once the water has reached temperature Ta. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (∫ H in (Cw*(T0-Tr))..(Cw*(Ta-Tr)), 1/(P - h*(H/Cw))) +
  ∫ H in (Cw*(Ta-Tr))..((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D), 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

lemma boilTime_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hCs : 0 ≤ Cs) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hh : 0 ≤ h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P) (hTr : Tr ≤ T0) (h1 : T0 ≤ Ta1) (h12 : Ta1 ≤ Ta2) (h2 : Ta2 ≤ Tb) :
    boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 := by
  have hC : 0 < Cw + Cs := by linarith
  set c := Cw*(T0-Tr) with hc
  set a := Cw*(Ta1-Tr) with ha
  set b := Cw*(Ta2-Tr) with hb
  set e := (Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D with he
  have hca : c ≤ a := by rw [hc, ha]; nlinarith
  have hab : a ≤ b := by rw [ha, hb]; nlinarith
  have hbe : b ≤ e := by
    rw [hb, he]; nlinarith [mul_nonneg hCs (show 0 ≤ Tb - Tr by linarith)]
  have ha0 : 0 ≤ a := by rw [ha]; nlinarith
  -- positivity of denominators
  have hpw : ∀ H, H ≤ b → 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; rw [hb] at hH; nlinarith
    nlinarith
  have hps : ∀ H, H ≤ e → 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Tb - Tr := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; rw [he] at hH; nlinarith
    nlinarith
  have cw : ContinuousOn (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*(H/Cw))) (Set.Icc c b) := by
    apply ContinuousOn.div continuousOn_const
    · fun_prop
    · intro H hH; exact (hpw H hH.2).ne'
  have cs : ContinuousOn (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) (Set.Icc a e) := by
    apply ContinuousOn.div continuousOn_const
    · fun_prop
    · intro H hH; exact (hps H hH.2).ne'
  have iw : ∀ x y, c ≤ x → x ≤ y → y ≤ b →
      IntervalIntegrable (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*(H/Cw))) MeasureTheory.volume x y := by
    intro x y hx hxy hy
    apply ContinuousOn.intervalIntegrable
    rw [Set.uIcc_of_le hxy]
    exact cw.mono (Set.Icc_subset_Icc hx hy)
  have is : ∀ x y, a ≤ x → x ≤ y → y ≤ e →
      IntervalIntegrable (fun H : ℝ => 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) MeasureTheory.volume x y := by
    intro x y hx hxy hy
    apply ContinuousOn.intervalIntegrable
    rw [Set.uIcc_of_le hxy]
    exact cs.mono (Set.Icc_subset_Icc hx hy)
  unfold boilTime
  have s1 := intervalIntegral.integral_add_adjacent_intervals
    (iw c a hc.le hca (hab.trans le_rfl)) (iw a b hca hab le_rfl)
  have s2 := intervalIntegral.integral_add_adjacent_intervals
    (is a b le_rfl hab hbe) (is b e hab le_rfl le_rfl)
  have key : (∫ H in a..b, 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) ≤ ∫ H in a..b, 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) := by
    apply intervalIntegral.integral_mono_on hab (is a b le_rfl hab hbe) (iw a b hca hab le_rfl)
    intro H hH
    have hpos := hpw H hH.2
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le hpos
    have : (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw := by
      rw [div_le_div_iff₀ hC hCw]
      nlinarith [mul_nonneg hCs (show 0 ≤ H by linarith [hH.1]), mul_nonneg hCw.le hD]
    nlinarith
  rw [← s1, ← s2]
  linarith

/-- ANSWER.  Under the model, the time to boil never decreases if the salt is added later
(at a hotter water temperature Ta ∈ [T0, Tb]); hence adding it at the start (Ta = T0 = 20 °C)
is optimal. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 ≤ Cs → 0 ≤ D → 0 ≤ h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 → T0 ≤ Tb →
    (∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
    (∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr hT
  refine ⟨fun a b h1 h12 h2 => boilTime_mono _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr h1 h12 h2, ?_⟩
  intro Ta hTa hTb
  exact boilTime_mono _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ _ hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr le_rfl hTa hTb

ANSWER (conditional): add the salt at the start (water at 20 C, t = 0). In the model below the
time to boil never decreases the later the salt goes in, so no heating delay helps. The gain is
small: the salt's effect on boiling time comes through heat losses only.

WHY (physics). Boiling point of the final solution Tb = 100 C + elevation (colligative; set by salt
amount alone). Energy needed to reach it from the start is fixed whatever the timing. Timing
matters only through heat loss: the stove delivers P, the pot loses h(T-Tr). Salt raises the heat
capacity, so for the same stored energy the pot is cooler, loses less, and heats faster. Hence
salt in early = less loss = earlier boil. If losses were zero (h=0) timing would not matter.
Common belief "salt later because salt makes water heat slower" is wrong about total time:
the energy must be supplied anyway. Adding salt to near-boiling water also risks boil-over (safety, not time).

FACTS SUPPLIED: 1 L water, 20 C, stove heating, salt to be added, aim = earliest boil of salted water;
salt amount, pot, heater, room unspecified.

MODEL CHOICES (my assumptions, for review):
 1 Lumped system, enthalpy coordinate H from "all at room temperature Tr"; water T = Tr+H/Cw (Cw includes pot).
 2 Constant heater power P; Newtonian loss h(T-Tr), h>0; evaporation ignored.
 3 Salt at Tr, mixed instantly, dissolves instantly; H conserved at mixing.
 4 Salt solution heat capacity Cw+Cs, Cs>=0; constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D>=0 (NaCl ~ +3.9 kJ/mol).
 5 Tb independent of timing; P > h(Tb-Tr) (boiling reachable); Tr <= 20 C; salt added before boiling (Ta in [T0,Tb]).
 6 Time from H1 to H2 = integral dH/(P - h(T(H)-Tr)); closed form via log antiderivatives Fw, Fs
   (Mathlib in this harness lacks interval integrals); derivative checked in Lean.
Not determined by the data: salt amount, P, h, Cw, Cs, D, Tr. The conclusion holds for all values satisfying the
assumptions, so it does not need them; the MAGNITUDE of the gain does and is not claimed (no calculation or measurement run).

LEAN (Model.lean): boilTime = total time as a function of Ta (water temperature when salt is added).
G_mono proves via derivative (1/dw - 1/ds >= 0, using the proved inequality (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) <= H/Cw for H>=0)
that Fw-Fs is monotone. answer_theorem: boilTime is monotone in Ta on [T0,Tb] and boilTime(T0) is the minimum.
Check passed, axioms only propext/Classical.choice/Quot.sound, no sorry. Not proved: strictness, magnitude,
validity of the premises, case h=0 (equal times), Tr>T0.
Sources: none external (general thermodynamics; no requests made).
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, heat capacity Cw+Cs, constant endothermic dissolution enthalpy D ≥ 0:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hCs : 0 ≤ Cs) (hD : 0 ≤ D) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) (Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) := by
  have hC : 0 < Cw + Cs := by linarith
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hle : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw := by
    intro H hH
    rw [div_le_div_iff₀ hC hCw]
    nlinarith [mul_nonneg hCs hH.1, mul_nonneg hCw.le hD]
  have hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) := by
    intro H hH
    have := hle H hH
    have := hw H hH
    nlinarith
  have hd : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
    intro H hH
    have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
      have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
      simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
    have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
      have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
      simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
    have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
    have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
    have := l1.sub l2
    convert this using 1
    · funext x; rfl
    · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
      have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
      have e3 := hh.ne'
      have e4 := hCw.ne'
      have e5 := hC.ne'
      field_simp
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg (convex_Icc _ _)
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H hH).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (interior_subset hH)).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H hH').deriv]
    have := hle H hH'
    have p1 := hw H hH'
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le p1
    nlinarith

/-- ANSWER.  In the model, with salt of fixed amount (Cs, D, Tb fixed), the time to boil
never decreases if the salt is added later, i.e. at a hotter water temperature Ta ∈ [T0, Tb];
hence adding it at the very start (Ta = T0 = 20 °C) is optimal. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 ≤ Cs → 0 ≤ D → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
    (∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
        boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hCs hD hh hP hTr
  have mono : ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 := by
    intro Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
    have G := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hCs hD hh hP
    have m := G (a := Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (b := Cw*(Ta2-Tr))
      ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]
    simp only at m
    linarith
  exact ⟨mono, fun Ta h1 h2 => mono T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2⟩

The search didn't return the specific numbers you asked for. It did return a few usable pieces and some pointers to where the numbers are.

**What the results give**
- **Infinite-dilution heat capacity.** Abraham and Marcus suggested a partial molar heat capacity of NaCl at infinite dilution of about –84 J·K⁻¹·mol⁻¹. The snippet is garbled, so I'm only moderately confident of that figure.
- **Enthalpy of solution at 25 °C.** A tutorial table lists NaCl(s) at +3.88 kJ/mol, so dissolving is slightly endothermic.
- **Temperature dependence.** In the recent Hückel-type model, the relevant parameters have quadratic temperature dependences. The heat-of-solution and heat-capacity values are therefore tied together through the temperature derivative of the enthalpy.

**Not found**
- The apparent molar heat capacity at 1 mol/kg and 25 °C.
- Numerical values for the temperature dependence of the enthalpy of solution.

**Sources that should contain the data**
- Perron, Fortier and Desnoyers, J. Chem. Thermodyn. 1975, 7, 1177. It covers apparent molar heat capacities and volumes of aqueous NaCl from 0.01 to 3 mol·kg⁻¹ between 274.65 and 318.15 K.
- Gates, Tillett, White and Wood, J. Chem. Thermodyn. 1987, 19, 131. It covers 0.05 to 3.0 mol·kg⁻¹, 350 to 600 K, and 2 to 18 MPa.
- The 2023–24 J. Solution Chem. papers (Springer, s10953-023-01322-y and s10953-024-01412-5), which give enthalpy and heat-capacity tables for NaCl(aq).
- The NIST/JPCRD review of NaCl(aq) thermodynamic properties (srd.nist.gov/jpcrdreprint/1.555660.pdf).

If you need the numbers themselves, I can search again for the Pitzer or Archer values at 25 °C.

Bash output
  "verdict": "proved",
assistant
I've revised the model and the check passes (verdict "proved"), but the new result gives no single best temperature for adding the salt. That is the honest outcome of dropping the Cs ≥ 0 assumption.

**The sign of Cs.** Cs ≥ 0 is probably false. Dissolved NaCl seems to have a negative partial molar heat capacity, about −84 J/K/mol at infinite dilution. That figure comes from a search snippet that was itself garbled, so I have not checked it against the primary source. The same search gave a dissolution enthalpy of +3.88 kJ/mol at 25 °C, so dissolving is endothermic. Because of the heat-capacity sign, the solution also holds slightly less heat per degree than the same water without salt. I withdrew the earlier claim that adding salt at the start always wins.

**What changed in the model.** Cs (the change in total heat capacity when the salt goes in) and D (the enthalpy of solution) can now have either sign. The only requirement is that the total heat capacity Cw+Cs stays positive. The other modelling choices are unchanged. I also added one premise: the stove can overcome heat losses at every state the pot passes through.

**What `answer_theorem` proves (in Lean, no `sorry`).** Adding salt makes the temperature jump by −(D + Cs·(Ta−Tr))/(Cw+Cs), where Ta is the water temperature at the moment of adding. Tr is room temperature, and Cw is the heat capacity of pot plus water.
- If salt addition cools the water everywhere between Ta1 and Ta2, adding at Ta1 is no slower than adding at Ta2.
- If it warms the water everywhere between Ta1 and Ta2, adding at Ta2 is no slower.

**What this means for your question.** With Cs < 0 the sign flips at Ta* = Tr + D/|Cs|, which is roughly 66 °C using the unverified constants. That lies inside the 20–100 °C heating range, so the model cannot name one best temperature. The result also depends on the salt amount and the heater-to-loss ratio, neither of which you gave. The effect is small, about 1 K of temperature jump per mole of salt in 1 L. In practice timing barely matters; adding salt early also avoids boil-over.

**Not proved.**
- That the best moment is at one end of the range, either 20 °C or just before boiling, rather than somewhere in the middle.
- Any size of the gain.
- Temperature dependence of D and Cs.

I made no measurement or operator request this cycle. Primary-source numbers would come from Perron, Fortier and Desnoyers (J. Chem. Thermodyn. 1975) or the NIST/JPCRD NaCl review.

I have not self-approved; this is for your review.

Files are in /work:
- Model.lean
- report.txt
- candidate.json

attempt-002/Model.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- ANSWER 1 (sign-free).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith

/-! Conclusions (statements of the proved theorems). -/
def conclusion_answer_theorem : Prop := ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ), 0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 → (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) → ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb → ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) → boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧ ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) → boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1)

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- ANSWER 1 (sign-free).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have := hH.1; rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw] at *; nlinarith [this]
    · have := hH.2; nlinarith [div_le_iff₀ hCw |>.mpr (by nlinarith : H ≤ (Ta2 - Tr) * Cw)]
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith

REVISION (cycle 2). Reviewer: do not assume Cs>=0; justify the sign of the heat-capacity change.
Finding: the premise Cs>=0 is probably FALSE. Dissolved NaCl has a NEGATIVE partial molar heat capacity
(about -84 J/K/mol at infinite dilution, Abraham & Marcus as reported by a web search snippet; not verified
against the primary source), so 1 kg of water plus salt has slightly LESS total heat capacity than 1 kg of
water plus the pot. Dissolution is endothermic at 25 C (+3.88 kJ/mol, tutorial table), and the enthalpy of
solution falls with temperature because Cp(dissolved) < Cp(solid NaCl, ~50 J/K/mol). I did not obtain
primary-source numbers (Perron-Fortier-Desnoyers 1975; NIST/JPCRD NaCl review are the places to look).
So the earlier claim "salt early always wins" is withdrawn. Cs and D are now of arbitrary sign (only Cw+Cs>0).

MODEL (choices, all mine): lumped pot+water heat capacity Cw; constant stove power P; Newton loss h(T-Tr);
no evaporation; salt at Tr dissolves instantly, enthalpy H conserved at mixing; solution temperature
Tr+(H-D)/(Cw+Cs) with D = enthalpy of solution at Tr, Cs = total heat-capacity change, constants;
final boiling point Tb fixed by salt amount; Ta in [T0,Tb]; stove can always overcome losses at all visited
states (premise hs). Facts supplied: 1 L water, 20 C, stove, salt later; salt amount, pot, heater, room unspecified.

RESULT (answer_theorem, proved in Lean, no sorry): time to boil as a function of the temperature Ta at which
salt goes in. Adding salt makes the temperature jump by -(D+Cs(Ta-Tr))/(Cw+Cs).
 - If between Ta1<=Ta2 salt addition always cools (D+Cs(x-Tr)>=0), adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2.
 - If it always warms (<=0), adding at Ta2 is no slower.
With Cs<0 the switch is at Ta* = Tr + D/|Cs|. Rough illustration with the (unverified) constants above:
Ta* ~ 20+3880/84 ~ 66 C. So the sign changes inside the 20-100 C range and the model gives NO unconditional
single best temperature. In a constant-parameter crude estimate with weak losses the early end looked
slightly better, but that was NOT proved and depends on h/P and the salt amount, which are unspecified.
Honest answer: the effect is tiny (a temperature jump of ~1 K per mole of salt in 1 L); the model cannot
decide it without salt amount, h/P and temperature-dependent data. Practically: time to boil is
dominated by the energy needed, so timing hardly matters; add salt early to avoid boil-over.
Not proved: endpoint-optimality (minimum is at 20 C or at the end, not interior, when Cs<0), magnitudes,
constants truth, temperature dependence of D and Cs. No measurement or request run.

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

- At 25 °C, 20 wt% NaCl has a specific heat of about 3411 J/kg·K. That is about 3.41 J/g·K.
- At 25 °C, 24 wt% NaCl has a specific heat of about 3314 J/kg·K. That is about 3.31 J/g·K.
- The page also has a table with columns at 2, 4, 6 … 24 wt%. One row shows 3728 J/kg·K at 10 °C for what looks like the 10 wt% column. I couldn't confirm that mapping, and the 25 °C values for 5% and 10% weren't in the extract.

**Other sources:**
- The NIST/JPCRD paper on aqueous NaCl thermodynamic properties (srd.nist.gov/jpcrdreprint/1.555709.pdf) is the rigorous source. It gives heat capacity as a function of molality, temperature and pressure, not as a mass-percent table.
- Wikipedia's NaCl data page gives 0.853 J/(g·K) for solid NaCl, which isn't the solution value.

**Rough interpolation (my estimate, not from a source):** 1% is about 4.1 J/g·K, 5% about 3.9, and 10% about 3.7. Check these against the ProcessConvert table or the NIST paper before relying on them.

The search turned up a few useful sources, but no single table covering 25–100 °C.

- **Infinite-dilution values from a DTIC report:** The most concrete numbers come from a DTIC report (ADA045707). Its table lists apparent molal heat capacities of NaCl extrapolated to zero molality in cal/mole-deg: −38.0 at 5 °C, −21.1 at 25 °C, −15.5 at 45 °C, −14.3 at 65 °C and −15.4 at 85 °C. These values are negative and become less negative with temperature, reaching a minimum magnitude near 65 °C. The report is a table of experimental values. The OCR is messy, so check the original before relying on the numbers.
- **Pitzer form at 25 °C:** The Pitzer equation for apparent molal heat capacity is φCp = C̄p,2° + (AJ/1.2) ln(1 + 1.2 I^1/2) − 2RT²[mB^J_MX + m²C^J_MX]. This is from a paper that fits 1–1 electrolytes at 25 °C (J. Phys. Chem., jp951355l). Here C̄p,2° is the infinite-dilution term, and B^J and C^J are ion-interaction parameters.
- **Wider temperature range:** A Journal of Solution Chemistry paper (Aalto/d-nb copies) tabulates relative apparent molar heat capacities of NaCl and uses Abraham and Marcus values for the infinite-dilution partial molar heat capacity. Another is Gates, Tillett, White and Wood, which covers 350–600 K and 0.05–3.0 mol·kg⁻¹. A Thermochimica Acta paper (1997, 294, 157–163) gives Pitzer parameters for NaCl–water between 25 and 100 °C. A separate paper (JACS, ja01476a013) gives standard partial molal heat capacities of NaCl from 0 to 100 °C.

I didn't find a ready-made table of Pitzer Cp parameters (β^J, C^J) at each temperature from 25 to 100 °C. The Thermochimica Acta paper and Archer's NaCl–water work (J. Phys. Chem. Ref. Data 21, 793, 1992) are the likeliest places to get them.

The revised candidate is in `/work/candidate.json` and the check tool returns "proved" with no errors. It has no `sorry` and no added axioms. The sign of Cs is now established from literature data rather than assumed. It is negative for cooking amounts and positive for strong brines. The data are not primary measurements.

**Sign of Cs** (the heat-capacity change when salt is added, in J/K per kg of water):
- **Source:** I computed Cs from the NaCl(aq) specific heats tabulated at processconvert.com, which come from the Laliberté (2009) correlation. These are computed values, not raw measurements, and I could not open the primary source.
- **Cooking amounts (negative):** at 25 °C, 2 wt% gives about −21 J/K, 4 wt% about −33 and 10 wt% about −32. At 80 °C the values are −12, −19 and −11.
- **Strong brines (positive):** 20 wt% gives about +84 J/K at 25 °C and +98 at 80 °C.
- **Sign change:** Cs changes sign near 13 wt%, and it is less negative when the water is hot.
- **Cross-check:** the infinite-dilution values are also negative, about −88 J/K/mol at 25 °C. That comes from an OCR'd DTIC table (ADA045707) and an Abraham & Marcus search snippet, both unverified.
- **Size of the effect:** for a 2 wt% solution (about 20 g of salt in 1 L), Cs is about −20 J/K. That is roughly 0.5% of the water's heat capacity.

**Lean theorems** (the accepted Newtonian lumped heating model is unchanged):
- **`cs_sign_theorem`:** proves Cs < 0 exactly when c/cw < 1 − w, where c and cw are the specific heats of the solution and of pure water and w is the salt mass fraction. It also proves the sign at 2, 4, 10 and 20 wt% at 25 °C and 80 °C. The tabulated c values are inputs, not derived.
- **`answer_theorem`:** if the enthalpy of solution is non-negative at the start and still non-negative at the boiling point, adding salt at the start is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature. That condition holds automatically when Cs is positive. The earlier sign-free comparison theorem is now a lemma that `answer_theorem` builds on.

**Implication for timing:** add the salt at the start. This is the best choice for strong brines and for cooking amounts at the numbers I have. For cooking amounts the condition is marginal near the boiling point:
- With Cs held at its 25 °C value, the switch temperature falls near 85 °C for a 2 wt% solution and above 100 °C for 4 wt%.
- With a temperature-dependent Cs, the enthalpy of solution stays about non-negative up to roughly 100 °C. This is a rough estimate, and I did not resolve the sign in the last few degrees.
- Either way, the temperature jump from adding salt is at most about 0.3 K, so the effect on boiling time is negligible. Adding salt early also avoids boil-over.

**Not proved:**
- Whether the condition holds for a given salt amount. The enthalpy of solution (about +3.9 kJ/mol, from an unverified tutorial table) and the constant-Cs values are literature inputs, and in reality both depend on temperature.
- Any quantitative difference in boiling time.

I made no request to you this cycle and ran no measurement. If you want the last-few-degrees sign settled, the temperature-dependent enthalpy of solution and Cp(T, m) from the NIST/JPCRD NaCl review (Archer 1992) would do it. I have not self-approved this result.

attempt-003/Model.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-- Total heat capacity added by the salt, for 1 kg water plus salt of mass fraction w, solution specific
 heat c and pure-water specific heat cw (J/g/K): Cs = 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw  (J/K). -/
noncomputable def saltCs (w c cw : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw

/-- SIGN OF Cs. (i) In general Cs<0 iff c/cw < 1-w (the solution's specific heat falls by more than the
 dilution by mass).  (ii) With the Laliberte-correlation values for NaCl(aq) (web-tabulated; computed, not a
 measurement; c in J/g/K, cw = 4.180 at 25 C, 4.197 at 80 C): Cs<0 for 2, 4, 10 wt% at 25 C and 80 C,
 and Cs>0 for 20 wt% at both. So Cs is negative in the cooking regime and changes sign near 13 wt%. -/
theorem cs_sign_theorem :
    (∀ w c cw : ℝ, 0 < cw → w < 1 → (saltCs w c cw < 0 ↔ c/cw < 1 - w)) ∧
    saltCs 0.02 4.076 4.180 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.04 3.981 4.180 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.10 3.733 4.180 < 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.20 3.411 4.180 > 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.02 4.101 4.197 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.04 4.011 4.197 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.10 3.767 4.197 < 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.20 3.436 4.197 > 0 := by
  refine ⟨?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
  · intro w c cw hcw hw
    have h1 : 0 < 1 - w := by linarith
    unfold saltCs
    rw [div_lt_iff₀ hcw]
    constructor <;> intro h
    · have : 1000/(1-w)*c < 1000*cw := by linarith
      rw [div_mul_eq_mul_div, div_lt_iff₀ h1] at this
      nlinarith
    · have : 1000*c < 1000*cw*(1-w) := by nlinarith
      have h2 : 1000/(1-w)*c < 1000*cw := by
        rw [div_mul_eq_mul_div, div_lt_iff₀ h1]; nlinarith
      linarith
  all_goals (unfold saltCs; norm_num)

/-- ANSWER.  Same accepted model (lumped Cw, constant power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation, salt added at Tr,
 enthalpy conserved), Cs of either sign.  D = enthalpy of solution at Tr (literature: D>0, endothermic,
 about +3.9 kJ/mol).  If D >= 0 and the enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0,
 i.e. D + Cs (Tb-Tr) >= 0 (Cs<0: cooling persists up to Tb; Cs>=0: automatic), then adding the salt at the
 start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    0 ≤ D → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(Tb-Tr) → T0 ≤ Tb →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs hD hDb hT0 Ta h1 h2
  refine (compare_theorem Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  rcases le_total 0 Cs with hc | hc
  · nlinarith
  · nlinarith
attempt-003/extracted.lean

/-! Conclusions (statements of the proved theorems). -/
def conclusion_answer_theorem : Prop := ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ), 0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 → (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) → 0 ≤ D → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(Tb-Tr) → T0 ≤ Tb → ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb → boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta

    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :

      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by

    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))

    have e4 := hCw.ne'

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)

    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
