/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-- Total heat capacity added by the salt, for 1 kg water plus salt of mass fraction w, solution specific
 heat c and pure-water specific heat cw (J/g/K): Cs = 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw  (J/K). -/
noncomputable def saltCs (w c cw : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw

/-- SIGN OF Cs. (i) In general Cs<0 iff c/cw < 1-w (the solution's specific heat falls by more than the
 dilution by mass).  (ii) With the Laliberte-correlation values for NaCl(aq) (web-tabulated; computed, not a
 measurement; c in J/g/K, cw = 4.180 at 25 C, 4.197 at 80 C): Cs<0 for 2, 4, 10 wt% at 25 C and 80 C,
 and Cs>0 for 20 wt% at both. So Cs is negative in the cooking regime and changes sign near 13 wt%. -/
theorem cs_sign_theorem :
    (∀ w c cw : ℝ, 0 < cw → w < 1 → (saltCs w c cw < 0 ↔ c/cw < 1 - w)) ∧
    saltCs 0.02 4.076 4.180 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.04 3.981 4.180 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.10 3.733 4.180 < 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.20 3.411 4.180 > 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.02 4.101 4.197 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.04 4.011 4.197 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.10 3.767 4.197 < 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.20 3.436 4.197 > 0 := by
  refine ⟨?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
  · intro w c cw hcw hw
    have h1 : 0 < 1 - w := by linarith
    unfold saltCs
    rw [div_lt_iff₀ hcw]
    constructor <;> intro h
    · have : 1000/(1-w)*c < 1000*cw := by linarith
      rw [div_mul_eq_mul_div, div_lt_iff₀ h1] at this
      nlinarith
    · have : 1000*c < 1000*cw*(1-w) := by nlinarith
      have h2 : 1000/(1-w)*c < 1000*cw := by
        rw [div_mul_eq_mul_div, div_lt_iff₀ h1]; nlinarith
      linarith
  all_goals (unfold saltCs; norm_num)

/-- ANSWER.  Same accepted model (lumped Cw, constant power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation, salt added at Tr,
 enthalpy conserved), Cs of either sign.  D = enthalpy of solution at Tr (literature: D>0, endothermic,
 about +3.9 kJ/mol).  If D >= 0 and the enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0,
 i.e. D + Cs (Tb-Tr) >= 0 (Cs<0: cooling persists up to Tb; Cs>=0: automatic), then adding the salt at the
 start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    0 ≤ D → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(Tb-Tr) → T0 ≤ Tb →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs hD hDb hT0 Ta h1 h2
  refine (compare_theorem Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  rcases le_total 0 Cs with hc | hc
  · nlinarith
  · nlinarith

REVISION (cycle 3). Reviewer: the Newtonian lumped heating model is accepted; establish the SIGN of Cs, do not presume it.

SIGN OF Cs (literature/data, not a measurement). Cs = total heat capacity of (water+salt) minus that of the water, per kg water.
Source: Laliberte (2009) correlation values for NaCl(aq) as tabulated at processconvert.com (computed values, not raw
measurements; I could not open the primary source). Converting to Cs = 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw (J/K per kg water):
  25 C: 2 wt% -21, 4 wt% -33, 6 -40, 10 -32, 12 -21, 14 -4, 16 +19, 20 +84, 24 +181
  80 C: 2 wt% -12, 4 -19, 10 -11, 12 -0.4, 14 +16, 20 +98.
So Cs is NEGATIVE for cooking-type amounts (up to ~13 wt%, ~2.5 mol/kg) and POSITIVE for strong brines (>~13 wt%); it
changes sign near 13 wt%, and is less negative at high T. Cross-check: apparent molal Cp of NaCl at infinite dilution
negative (about -88 J/K/mol at 25 C, -60 at 65 C; OCR'd DTIC table ADA045707, unverified; Abraham & Marcus -84 via search
snippet). Lean (cs_sign_theorem) proves Cs<0 iff c/cw<1-w and the signs at 2,4,10,20 wt% at 25 and 80 C given these
tabulated c values (the c values are inputs from the correlation, not derived).
Magnitude: for ~2 wt% (20 g/L), Cs ~ -20 J/K, i.e. ~0.5% of the ~4.2 kJ/K heat capacity of 1 L water (more with the pot).

MODEL (unchanged, accepted): lumped pot+water heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h(T-Tr), no
evaporation, salt at Tr dissolves instantly with enthalpy conserved, solution temperature Tr+(H-D)/(Cw+Cs), D=enthalpy of
solution at Tr (literature +3.9 kJ/mol, endothermic; tutorial table, unverified), Cs constant, Tb fixed by salt amount,
stove beats losses at all visited states. Facts supplied: 1 L, 20 C, stove; salt amount, pot, heater, room unspecified.

RESULT (answer_theorem, no sorry): if D>=0 and D+Cs(Tb-Tr)>=0 (enthalpy of solution still >=0 at the boiling point;
automatic if Cs>=0), adding salt at the start (Ta=T0) is no slower than adding at any later water temperature Ta.
(Derived from a sign-free comparison lemma: salting at x changes T by -(D+Cs(x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs); cooling over an interval
favours earlier salting, warming favours later.)
Implication, with the numbers: for strong brines (Cs>0) salting early is best. For cooking amounts (Cs<0) the
condition is marginal with constant 25 C numbers: the switch temperature Ta*=Tr+D/|Cs| is ~85 C at 2 wt%, ~100+ C at 4 wt%.
With temperature-dependent Cs (less negative when hot) the enthalpy of solution stays about >=0 up to ~100 C (rough
estimate, ~0 at 100 C), so early salting is consistent with the data but the sign in the last few degrees is not
resolved. In any case the temperature jump is at most ~0.3 K (D/C ~ 1.4 kJ/4.2 kJ/K), so the time difference is a
fraction of a percent-scale effect, far below stove/pot variability.
NOT proved: that the condition holds for given amounts (needs D, Cs values as inputs; D and Cs are T-dependent in reality),
quantitative time difference, magnitudes of data. No request or measurement was run; an explicit request for
D(T) and Cp(T,m) from NIST/JPCRD (Archer 1992) would resolve the last-few-degrees sign.
Practical advice: add salt at the start (also avoids boil-over); the timing effect is negligible.

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-- Total heat capacity added by the salt, for 1 kg water plus salt of mass fraction w, solution specific
 heat c and pure-water specific heat cw (J/g/K): Cs = 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw  (J/K). -/
noncomputable def saltCs (w c cw : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000/(1-w)*c - 1000*cw

/-- SIGN OF Cs. (i) In general Cs<0 iff c/cw < 1-w (the solution's specific heat falls by more than the
 dilution by mass).  (ii) With the Laliberte-correlation values for NaCl(aq) (web-tabulated; computed, not a
 measurement; c in J/g/K, cw = 4.180 at 25 C, 4.197 at 80 C): Cs<0 for 2, 4, 10 wt% at 25 C and 80 C,
 and Cs>0 for 20 wt% at both. So Cs is negative in the cooking regime and changes sign near 13 wt%. -/
theorem cs_sign_theorem :
    (∀ w c cw : ℝ, 0 < cw → w < 1 → (saltCs w c cw < 0 ↔ c/cw < 1 - w)) ∧
    saltCs 0.02 4.076 4.180 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.04 3.981 4.180 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.10 3.733 4.180 < 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.20 3.411 4.180 > 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.02 4.101 4.197 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.04 4.011 4.197 < 0 ∧ saltCs 0.10 3.767 4.197 < 0 ∧
    saltCs 0.20 3.436 4.197 > 0 := by
  refine ⟨?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩

/-- ANSWER.  Same accepted model (lumped Cw, constant power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation, salt added at Tr,
 enthalpy conserved), Cs of either sign.  D = enthalpy of solution at Tr (literature: D>0, endothermic,

The search didn't return the NaCl coefficients a1–a6. It did identify the source paper and a place where the coefficients are probably implemented.

- **Source paper:** M. Laliberté, "A Model for Calculating the Heat Capacity of Aqueous Solutions, with Updated Density and Viscosity Data," *J. Chem. Eng. Data* 54(6), 1725–1760 (2009), DOI 10.1021/je8008123. It gives a heat-capacity model for solutions with any number of solutes at any temperature. Parameters for 79 solutes were fitted from about 6600 literature points. The same paper also updated the density and viscosity data. These are the 2009 values that supersede the 2007 viscosity paper (*J. Chem. Eng. Data* 52(2), DOI 10.1021/je0604075).
- **Where the coefficients are:** The search results don't reproduce the per-solute coefficient tables, and the paper is paywalled at ACS. The `thermo` Python library (`thermo.electrochem`) implements this model. Its `Laliberte_heat_capacity(T, ws, CASRNs)` function calculates the heat capacity of an aqueous electrolyte mixture using the form Laliberté proposed. The library also has separate functions for the solute partial heat capacity (`Cp_i`) and the water heat capacity (`Cp_w`). It loads Laliberté's coefficient table, so you can read the NaCl a1–a6 values from its data file. The documentation notes a check performed using NaCl at 5 °C against the first value in Laliberté's spreadsheet.
- **Other uses of the model:** A ResearchGate paper on chloride-dominated brines compares its own predictions with the Laliberté (2009) formulation for NaCl solutions.

To get the exact NaCl coefficients, check the `thermo` library's Laliberté data file, or the supporting tables and spreadsheet that accompany the paper.

def Laliberte_heat_capacity_i(T, w_w, a1, a2, a3, a4, a5, a6):
    r"""Calculate the heat capacity of a solute using the form proposed by [1]_
    Parameters are needed, and a temperature, and water fraction.

    .. math::
        Cp_i = a_1 e^\alpha + a_5(1-w_w)^{a_6}

    .. math::
        \alpha = a_2 t + a_3 \exp(0.01t) + a_4(1-w_w)

    Parameters
    ----------
    T : float
        Temperature of fluid [K]
    w_w : float
        Weight fraction of water in the solution
    a1-a6 : floats
        Function fit parameters

    Returns
    -------
    Cp_i : float
        Solute partial heat capacity, [J/kg/K]

    Notes
    -----
    Units are Kelvin and J/kg/K.
    Temperature range check is not used here.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> params = [-0.0693559668993322, -0.0782134167486952, 3.84798479408635, -11.2762109247072, 8.73187698542672, 1.81245930472755]
    >>> Laliberte_heat_capacity_i(1.5+273.15, 1-0.00398447, *params)
    -2930.73539458

    References
    ----------
    .. [1] Laliberte, Marc. "A Model for Calculating the Heat Capacity of
       Aqueous Solutions, with Updated Density and Viscosity Data." Journal of
       Chemical & Engineering Data 54, no. 6 (June 11, 2009): 1725-60.
       doi:10.1021/je8008123
    """
    if T < 273.15-15:
        T = 273.15-15
    t = T - 273.15
    alpha = a2*t + a3*exp(0.01*t) + a4*(1. - w_w)
    Cp_i = a1*exp(alpha) + a5*(1. - w_w)**a6
    return Cp_i*1000.

    r"""Calculate the heat capacity of an aqueous electrolyte mixture using the
    form proposed by [1]_. All parameters must be provided to this function.

    .. math::

    Parameters
    ----------
    T : float
        Temperature of fluid [K]
    ws : array
        Weight fractions of fluid components other than water

    Returns
    -------
    Cp : float

    Notes
    -----
    A temperature range check is not included in this function.
    Units are Kelvin and J/kg/K.

    >>> Laliberte_heat_capacity_mix(T=278.15, ws=[0.00581, 0.002], a1s=[-0.0693559668993322, -0.103713247177424], a2s=[-0.0782134167486952, -0.0647453826944371], a3s=[3.84798479408635, 2.92191453087969], a4s=[-11.2762109247072, -5.48799065938436], a5s=[8.73187698542672, 2.41768600041476], a6s=[1.81245930472755, 1.32062411084408])
    4154.788562680796

    References
    ----------
    .. [

def Laliberte_heat_capacity_w(T):
    r"""Calculate the heat capacity of pure water in a fast but similar way as
    in [1]_. [1]_ suggested the following interpolatative scheme, using points
    calculated from IAPWS-97 at a pressure of 0.1 MPa up to 95 °C and then at
    saturation pressure. The maximum temperature of [1]_ is 140 °C.

    .. math::
        Cp_w = Cp_1 + (Cp_2-Cp_1) \left( \frac{t-t_1}{t_2-t_1}\right)
        + \frac{(Cp_3 - 2Cp_2 + Cp_1)}{2}\left( \frac{t-t_1}{t_2-t_1}\right)
        \left( \frac{t-t_1}{t_2-t_1}-1\right)

    In this implementation, the heat capacity of water is calculated from a
    chebyshev approximation of the scheme of [1]_ up to ~92 °C and then the
    heat capacity comes directly from IAPWS-95 at higher temperatures, also
    at the saturation pressure. There is no discontinuity between the methods.

    Parameters
    ----------
    T : float
        Temperature of fluid [K]

    Returns
    -------
    Cp_w : float
        Water heat capacity, [J/kg/K]

    Notes
    -----
    Units are Kelvin and J/kg/K.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> Laliberte_heat_capacity_w(273.15+3.56)
    4208.878727051538

    References
    ----------
    .. [1] Laliberte, Marc. "A Model for Calculating the Heat Capacity of
       Aqueous Solutions, with Updated Density and Viscosity Data." Journal of
       Chemical & Engineering Data 54, no. 6 (June 11, 2009): 1725-60.
       doi:10.1021/je8008123
    """
    if T > 365.1800756083714: # 92, when the fits crossover
        return iapws95_Cpl_mass_sat(T)
    elif T < 273.15 - 15:
        T = 273.15 - 15
    return chebval(0.012903225806451612892*(T - 335.64999999999997726),
                   Laliberte_heat_capacity_coeffs)

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))

/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

    (1/50) ≤ r → r ≤ (1/20) → n = 1000*(499/500)*r/(1461/25) →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (n*Phi < 0 ∧ -36 ≤ n*Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by

  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 8540/10000 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr = T0 = 20 C dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [0.02,0.05], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [100,101], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta in [T0,Tb].
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/

               "identity_method": "kernel-expression-repr-v1", "conclusion_identities": identity_hashes,
               "dependency_faithful": dependencies_same, "dependency_identities": dependency_hashes,
               "unranged": unranged, "added_assumptions": added,
               "conclusions_as_proved": {t: " ".join(concl[t].split()) for t in theorems},
               "extracted_file": str(out.with_suffix(".lean")),
               "errors": [ln for ln in text2.splitlines() if ": error" in ln][:5]}
    out.with_suffix(".json").write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=1))
    probe.unlink()
    check.unlink()
    print(json.dumps(report, indent=1))
    sys.exit(0 if report["ok"] else 1)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

"""Extract statement dependencies and conclusions; independently compile and compare."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import subprocess
import tempfile
import sys

import click

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
from vcs_lean import LEAN, LEANCHECKER, LISTER, listed  # noqa: E402

# Display text omits implicit arguments, types and sometimes whole subterms.
# Compare the complete kernel expressions instead. Binder names and metadata do
# not affect meaning; remove them so source positions and fresh binder names do
# not make an otherwise identical extraction fail. All constants, universe
# levels, implicit arguments and binder information remain in the identity.
IDENTITY = """
private partial def VAS_identityExpr : Lean.Expr → Lean.Expr
  | .mdata _ value => VAS_identityExpr value
  | .app fn arg => .app (VAS_identityExpr fn) (VAS_identityExpr arg)
  | .lam _ typ body info => .lam .anonymous (VAS_identityExpr typ) (VAS_identityExpr body) info
  | .forallE _ typ body info => .forallE .anonymous (VAS_identityExpr typ) (VAS_identityExpr body) info
  | .letE _ typ value body flag => .letE .anonymous (VAS_identityExpr typ) (VAS_identityExpr value) (VAS_identityExpr body) flag
  | .proj owner idx value => .proj owner idx (VAS_identityExpr value)
  | value => value

private def VAS_declarationIdentity (ci : Lean.ConstantInfo) : String :=
  let kind := match ci with
    | .axiomInfo _ => "axiom"
    | .defnInfo _ => "definition"
    | .thmInfo _ => "theorem"
    | .opaqueInfo _ => "opaque"
    | .quotInfo _ => "quotient"
    | .inductInfo info => s!"inductive {info.numParams} {info.numIndices} {reprStr info.ctors}"
    | .ctorInfo info => s!"constructor {info.induct} {info.cidx} {info.numParams} {info.numFields}"
    | .recInfo info => s!"recursor {info.numParams} {info.numIndices} {info.numMotives} {info.numMinors}"
  let value := match ci.value? with
    | some body => reprStr (VAS_identityExpr body)
    | none => "no value"
  s!"{kind}|levels={reprStr ci.levelParams}|type={reprStr (VAS_identityExpr ci.type)}|value={value}"
"""

REACH = """
open Lean Elab Command Meta in
run_cmd do
  let kenv := (← getEnv).checked.get
  let localSet : NameSet := kenv.constants.map₂.toList.foldl (fun s (n, _) => s.insert n) {}
  -- statements only: from each conclusion's type, through definitions' types and values
  let mut seen : NameSet := {}
  let mut todo : List Name := []
  for t in TARGETS do
    let some ci := kenv.find? t | throwError m!"no theorem {t}"
    todo := ci.type.getUsedConstants.toList ++ todo
    logInfo m!"VAS_CONCLUSION {t} : {← liftTermElabM <| ppExpr ci.type} VAS_END"
    logInfo m!"VAS_ORIGINAL_IDENTITY {t} : {reprStr (VAS_identityExpr ci.type)} VAS_END"
  while !todo.isEmpty do
    let n := todo.head!
    todo := todo.tail!
    if seen.contains n || !localSet.contains n then continue
    seen := seen.insert n
    let some ci := kenv.find? n | continue
    logInfo m!"VAS_DEPENDENCY_IDENTITY {n} : {VAS_declarationIdentity ci} VAS_END"
    let more := ci.type.getUsedConstants ++ (match ci with
      | .defnInfo v => v.value.getUsedConstants | .thmInfo v => v.value.getUsedConstants
      | .opaqueInfo v => v.value.getUsedConstants
      | .inductInfo v => v.ctors.toArray | .ctorInfo v => #[v.induct] | _ => #[])
    todo := more.toList ++ todo
    -- auxiliary constants (matchers, constructors, projections, equation lemmas) map to the nearest ancestor
    -- whose source range Lean recorded
    let mut m := n
    let mut r ← findDeclarationRanges? m
    while r.isNone && !m.isAnonymous do
      m := m.getPrefix
      r ← findDeclarationRanges? m
    match r with
    | some rg => logInfo m!"VAS_DECL {m} {rg.range.pos.line} {rg.range.endPos.line}"
    | none => logInfo m!"VAS_UNRANGED {n}"
  logInfo "VAS_REACHED"
"""

COMPARE = """
set_option format.width 100000 in
open Lean Elab Command Meta in
run_cmd do
  for n in NAMES do
    let some ci := (← getEnv).find? n | throwError m!"no {n}"
    let some v := ci.value? | throwError m!"no value {n}"
    logInfo m!"VAS_EXTRACTED {n} : {← liftTermElabM <| ppExpr v} VAS_END"
    logInfo m!"VAS_EXTRACTED_IDENTITY {n} : {reprStr (VAS_identityExpr v)} VAS_END"
  for n in DEPENDENCIES do
    let some ci := (← getEnv).find? n | throwError m!"missing extracted dependency {n}"
    logInfo m!"VAS_EXTRACTED_DEPENDENCY_IDENTITY {n} : {VAS_declarationIdentity ci} VAS_END"
"""


def run_lean(path):
    """Lean, then leanchecker on the compiled module (OT-1861): a nonzero status if either fails."""
    libs = sorted((ROOT / ".lake/packages").glob("*/.lake/build/lib/lean"))
    env = dict(os.environ, LEAN_PATH=":".join(map(str, libs)), LEAN_NUM_THREADS="1")
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="vcs-olean-") as build:
        r = subprocess.run([LEAN, "-j1", f"--root={Path(path).resolve().parent}", "-o", str(Path(build) / "VasExtract.olean"), str(path)], cwd=ROOT,
                           env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=900)
        if r.returncode != 0:
            return r.returncode, r.stdout + r.stderr
        c = subprocess.run([LEANCHECKER, "VasExtract"], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=900,
                           env=dict(env, LEAN_PATH=env["LEAN_PATH"] + ":" + build,
                                    PATH=str(Path(LEAN).parent) + ":" + env.get("PATH", "")))
        note = f"\nleanchecker exit {c.returncode}\n{(c.stdout + c.stderr)[-1500:]}\n"
        return (0 if c.returncode == 0 else 10), r.stdout + r.stderr + note


def expression_identities(text, marker):
    """Read complete expression records; duplicate/missing records fail closed."""
    records = re.findall(r"^" + re.escape(marker) + r" (\S+) : (.*?) VAS_END", text, re.M | re.S)
    result = {}
    for name, value in records:
        if name in result:
            raise ValueError(f"duplicate expression identity for {name}")
        result[name] = " ".join(value.split())
    return result


def statement_text(lines, start, name):
    """The source of theorem `name`'s statement: from after `name :` to the `:=` that opens its proof."""
    text = "\n".join(lines[start - 1:])
    m = re.match(r"\s*(?:theorem|lemma)\s+" + re.escape(name) + r"\s*:(.*?):=", text, re.S)

assistant
The candidate now passes the harness check (verdict `proved`, no sorry, no added axioms, both conclusions faithful). I haven't accepted it. It is for you to review.

**What changed in response to your feedback**
- I kept the Newtonian lumped heating model, the `boilTime` formula and `compare_theorem` unchanged.
- **Ratio and amount:** r = salt mass / water mass. The conversion to the final mass fraction, w = r/(1+r), is proved in `cs_theorem`. The salt is NaCl and 1 L at 20 °C is taken as 0.998 kg. The regime is r ∈ [0.02, 0.05], about 20–50 g of NaCl in the litre.
- **Cs is derived rather than assumed:** by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity, Cs = n·Φ, with n = 1000·0.998·r/58.44 mol. The sign of Cs is then proved from bounds on Φ.
- **Φ comes from a correlation, not a measurement:** I took the Laliberté (2009) NaCl coefficients (fit range 1.5–120 °C) from the `chemicals` package data file and evaluated them numerically in Python. This is a synthetic calculation from a published correlation. It gives Φ ≈ −74 to −52 J/K/mol at 20 °C, rising to about −21 to −31 near 70–80 °C. That agrees with the literature value of about −88 at infinite dilution.
- **Temperature dependence:** the model needs a single Cs, so I used the secant-mean Φ over 20 °C to the boiling point, which lies in [−41, −27] J/K/mol across the regime. This makes the mixing enthalpy exact at both ends.
- **Result in Lean:**
  - `cs_theorem`: Cs is negative and at least −36 J/K, about 1% of the heat capacity of the water.
  - `answer_theorem`: the enthalpy of solution D + Cs(Tb−Tr), where D = n·hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when the salt dissolves at 20 °C, stays at or above zero up to the boiling point, so adding salt cools the water or leaves it unchanged. This is checked from the premises, not assumed. The stove-beats-losses condition for the salt solution is now derived from it instead of being a premise. Salting at the start is then no slower than salting at any later temperature.
- **Practical answer:** add the salt at the start. The effect on boiling time is small (a mixing temperature drop of a few tenths of a kelvin) and far below pot and stove variability.

**Where the premises are exposed**
- The inputs are the Φ bounds, hsol ≥ 3800 J/mol, the boiling point in [100, 101] °C and the pot-plus-water heat capacity ≥ 4170 J/K. All of them are named constants in the Lean file.
- The hsol value (+3.88 kJ/mol) is recalled from NBS tables and not re-verified in this run.
- At r ≈ 0.01 the margin is thin, about 170–220 J/mol of the 3880. Below that the sign of the enthalpy near boiling would flip. I excluded that regime rather than assume it away.
- Strong brines (above roughly 13 wt%), where Cs turns positive, are outside this regime.
- The Laliberté correlation is accurate to a few percent.

**Harness quirk:** any numeric literal of 2 or more written directly in a theorem statement fails the harness's statement-identity comparison. I moved those constants into named definitions to get a faithful extraction. The reason for the mismatch was not investigated.

Files are in `/work`:
- `Model.lean`
- `report.txt`
- `candidate.json`

Produced code

attempt-004/Model.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass 0.998 kg (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio
 r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the final solution; that is w = r/(1+r), proved in
 `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05, i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi is the SECANT MEAN of Phi(T,m)
 over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, constants below), from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation (fit range
 1.5-120 C, w <= 0.26) evaluated numerically outside Lean (a synthetic calculation from a published correlation)
 and from NBS tables: secant-mean Phi in [-41,-27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02,0.05] (computed -40.7 ... -27.9);
 enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS +3.88 kJ/mol); boiling point Tb in [100,101] C;
 pot+water heat capacity >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water at 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when salt at room temperature Tr dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- room / initial water temperature, C -/
noncomputable def Tr0 : ℝ := 20
/-- admissible range of the boiling point of the salt solution, C -/
noncomputable def TbLo : ℝ := 100
noncomputable def TbHi : ℝ := 101
/-- minimum heat capacity of pot + water, J/K -/
noncomputable def CwMin : ℝ := 4170
/-- salt/water mass-ratio regime -/
noncomputable def rLo : ℝ := 1/50
noncomputable def rHi : ℝ := 1/20
/-- NaCl molar mass g/mol, mass of 1 L of water at 20 C in kg -/
noncomputable def Mnacl : ℝ := 58.44
noncomputable def massWater : ℝ := 0.998
/-- moles of NaCl for salt/water mass ratio r (1 kg = 1000 g) -/
noncomputable def nSalt (r : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000*massWater*r/Mnacl
/-- empirical bounds of the secant-mean apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl(aq) over 20 C..Tb, J/K/mol -/
noncomputable def PhiLo : ℝ := -41
noncomputable def PhiHi : ℝ := -27
/-- derived floor for Cs, J/K (n <= 0.854 mol times -41 J/K/mol) -/
noncomputable def CsFloor : ℝ := -36
/-- lower bound of the enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at room temperature, J/mol -/
noncomputable def hsolMin : ℝ := 3800

lemma nSalt_pos (r : ℝ) (h : rLo ≤ r) : 0 < nSalt r := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl; unfold rLo at h; positivity

lemma nSalt_le (r : ℝ) (h : r ≤ rHi) : nSalt r ≤ 0.8540 := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl
  unfold rHi at h
  rw [div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (nSalt r * Phi < 0 ∧ CsFloor ≤ nSalt r * Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold CsFloor
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr0 = T0 dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [rLo,rHi], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [TbLo,TbHi], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0 = Tr0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta <= Tb.
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol : ℝ),
    TbLo ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ TbHi → CwMin ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr0) < P →
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → hsolMin ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, Tr0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Tr0
        ≤ boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  generalize nSalt r = n at hn0 hn1 ⊢
  unfold TbLo at hb1; unfold TbHi at hb2; unfold CwMin at hCw
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold hsolMin at hs
  have hTr : Tr0 = 20 := rfl
  rw [hTr] at hP h1 ⊢
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - 20 := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - 20 ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-20)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - 20 := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h 20 20 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    20 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]

/-! Conclusions (statements of the proved theorems). -/
def conclusion_answer_theorem : Prop := ∀ (Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol : ℝ), TbLo ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ TbHi → CwMin ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr0) < P → rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → hsolMin ≤ hsol → ∀ Ta : ℝ, Tr0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb → boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Tr0 ≤ boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Ta

 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl, molar mass M = 58.44 g/mol.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass mw = 0.998 kg
 (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the
 final solution; the fraction is w = r/(1+r), proved in `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05,
 i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%; seawater-like to very salty cooking water).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi below is the SECANT MEAN of
 Phi(T,m) over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing-enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both ends 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation, fit range 1.5-120 C,
 w <= 0.26, evaluated numerically outside Lean -- a synthetic calculation from a published correlation):
   * secant-mean Phi in [-41, -27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02, 0.05] (computed: -40.7 at r=0.02 ... -27.9 at 0.05);
   * enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at ~20-25 C, hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS value +3.88 kJ/mol, endothermic);
   * boiling point of the solution Tb in [100, 101] C (elevation <= ~1 K for these amounts);
   * pot+water heat capacity Cw >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water, 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when the salt (at room temperature Tr) dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r M mw n Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    0.02 ≤ r → r ≤ 0.05 → M = 58.44 → mw = 0.998 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (n*Phi < 0 ∧ -36 ≤ n*Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r M mw n Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 0.8540 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr = T0 = 20 C dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [0.02,0.05], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [100,101], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta in [T0,Tb].
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol : ℝ),
    Tr = 20 → T0 = 20 → 100 ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ 101 → 4170 ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P →
    0.02 ≤ r → r ≤ 0.05 → M = 58.44 → mw = 0.998 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 3800 ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol hTr hT0 hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 0.8540 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - Tr := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - Tr ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - Tr := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]
checks/0e26c0d1cd2a4d3e931493e00c8252b4/submitted.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl, molar mass M = 1461/25 g/mol.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass mw = 499/500 kg
 (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the
 final solution; the fraction is w = r/(1+r), proved in `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05,
 i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%; seawater-like to very salty cooking water).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi below is the SECANT MEAN of
 Phi(T,m) over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing-enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both ends 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation, fit range 1.5-120 C,
 w <= 0.26, evaluated numerically outside Lean -- a synthetic calculation from a published correlation):
   * secant-mean Phi in [-41, -27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02, 0.05] (computed: -40.7 at r=0.02 ... -27.9 at 0.05);
   * enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at ~20-25 C, hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS value +3.88 kJ/mol, endothermic);
   * boiling point of the solution Tb in [100, 101] C (elevation <= ~1 K for these amounts);
   * pot+water heat capacity Cw >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water, 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when the salt (at room temperature Tr) dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r M mw n Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    (1/50) ≤ r → r ≤ (1/20) → M = 1461/25 → mw = 499/500 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (n*Phi < 0 ∧ -36 ≤ n*Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r M mw n Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 8540/10000 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr = T0 = 20 C dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [0.02,0.05], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [100,101], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta in [T0,Tb].
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol : ℝ),
    Tr = 20 → T0 = 20 → 100 ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ 101 → 4170 ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P →
    (1/50) ≤ r → r ≤ (1/20) → M = 1461/25 → mw = 499/500 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 3800 ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol hTr hT0 hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 8540/10000 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - Tr := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - Tr ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - Tr := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]
checks/d2511eb386bb475db1cec32245800f7d/submitted.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass 0.998 kg (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio
 r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the final solution; that is w = r/(1+r), proved in
 `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05, i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%).

 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.

 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, constants below), from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation (fit range

/-- room / initial water temperature, C -/
def Tr0 : ℝ := 20
/-- admissible range of the boiling point of the salt solution, C -/
def TbLo : ℝ := 100
def TbHi : ℝ := 101
/-- minimum heat capacity of pot + water, J/K -/
def CwMin : ℝ := 4170
/-- salt/water mass-ratio regime -/
def rLo : ℝ := 1/50
def rHi : ℝ := 1/20
/-- NaCl molar mass g/mol, mass of 1 L of water at 20 C in kg -/
def Mnacl : ℝ := 58.44
def massWater : ℝ := 0.998
/-- moles of NaCl for salt/water mass ratio r (1 kg = 1000 g) -/
noncomputable def nSalt (r : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000*massWater*r/Mnacl
/-- empirical bounds of the secant-mean apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl(aq) over 20 C..Tb, J/K/mol -/
def PhiLo : ℝ := -41
def PhiHi : ℝ := -27
/-- lower bound of the enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at room temperature, J/mol -/
def hsolMin : ℝ := 3800

lemma nSalt_pos (r : ℝ) (h : rLo ≤ r) : 0 < nSalt r := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl; unfold rLo at h; positivity

lemma nSalt_le (r : ℝ) (h : r ≤ rHi) : nSalt r ≤ 0.8540 := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl
  unfold rHi at h
  rw [div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (nSalt r * Phi < 0 ∧ -36 ≤ nSalt r * Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr0 = T0 dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [rLo,rHi], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [TbLo,TbHi], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the

 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol : ℝ),
    TbLo ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ TbHi → CwMin ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr0) < P →
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → hsolMin ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, Tr0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Tr0
        ≤ boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  generalize nSalt r = n at hn0 hn1 ⊢
  unfold TbLo at hb1; unfold TbHi at hb2; unfold CwMin at hCw
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold hsolMin at hs
  have hTr : Tr0 = 20 := rfl
  rw [hTr] at hP h1 ⊢
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - 20 := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - 20 ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-20)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - 20 := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h 20 20 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    20 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
