import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl, molar mass M = 58.44 g/mol.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass mw = 0.998 kg
 (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the
 final solution; the fraction is w = r/(1+r), proved in `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05,
 i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%; seawater-like to very salty cooking water).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi below is the SECANT MEAN of
 Phi(T,m) over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing-enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both ends 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation, fit range 1.5-120 C,
 w <= 0.26, evaluated numerically outside Lean -- a synthetic calculation from a published correlation):
   * secant-mean Phi in [-41, -27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02, 0.05] (computed: -40.7 at r=0.02 ... -27.9 at 0.05);
   * enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at ~20-25 C, hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS value +3.88 kJ/mol, endothermic);
   * boiling point of the solution Tb in [100, 101] C (elevation <= ~1 K for these amounts);
   * pot+water heat capacity Cw >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water, 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when the salt (at room temperature Tr) dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r M mw n Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    0.02 ≤ r → r ≤ 0.05 → M = 58.44 → mw = 0.998 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (n*Phi < 0 ∧ -36 ≤ n*Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r M mw n Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 0.8540 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr = T0 = 20 C dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [0.02,0.05], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [100,101], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta in [T0,Tb].
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol : ℝ),
    Tr = 20 → T0 = 20 → 100 ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ 101 → 4170 ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P →
    0.02 ≤ r → r ≤ 0.05 → M = 58.44 → mw = 0.998 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 3800 ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol hTr hT0 hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 0.8540 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - Tr := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - Tr ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - Tr := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2

    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'

    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by

    have := sgn H hH'

 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),

    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →

          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by

    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl, molar mass M = 58.44 g/mol.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass mw = 0.998 kg
 (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the
 final solution; the fraction is w = r/(1+r), proved in `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05,
 i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%; seawater-like to very salty cooking water).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi below is the SECANT MEAN of
 Phi(T,m) over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing-enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both ends 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation, fit range 1.5-120 C,
 w <= 0.26, evaluated numerically outside Lean -- a synthetic calculation from a published correlation):
   * secant-mean Phi in [-41, -27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02, 0.05] (computed: -40.7 at r=0.02 ... -27.9 at 0.05);
   * enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at ~20-25 C, hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS value +3.88 kJ/mol, endothermic);
   * boiling point of the solution Tb in [100, 101] C (elevation <= ~1 K for these amounts);
   * pot+water heat capacity Cw >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water, 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when the salt (at room temperature Tr) dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r M mw n Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    0.02 ≤ r → r ≤ 0.05 → M = 58.44 → mw = 0.998 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (n*Phi < 0 ∧ -36 ≤ n*Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r M mw n Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 0.8540 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr = T0 = 20 C dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [0.02,0.05], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [100,101], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta in [T0,Tb].
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol : ℝ),
    Tr = 20 → T0 = 20 → 100 ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ 101 → 4170 ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P →
    0.02 ≤ r → r ≤ 0.05 → M = 58.44 → mw = 0.998 → n = 1000*mw*r/M →
    -41 ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ -27 → 3800 ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb T0 ≤ boilTime Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tr T0 Tb r M mw n Phi hsol hTr hT0 hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hM hmw hn hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 : 0 < n := by rw [hn, hM, hmw]; positivity
  have hn1 : n ≤ 0.8540 := by
    rw [hn, hM, hmw, div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - Tr := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - Tr ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-Tr) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - Tr := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h Tr T0 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    T0 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass 0.998 kg (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio
 r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the final solution; that is w = r/(1+r), proved in
 `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05, i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi is the SECANT MEAN of Phi(T,m)
 over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, constants below), from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation (fit range
 1.5-120 C, w <= 0.26) evaluated numerically outside Lean (a synthetic calculation from a published correlation)
 and from NBS tables: secant-mean Phi in [-41,-27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02,0.05] (computed -40.7 ... -27.9);
 enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS +3.88 kJ/mol); boiling point Tb in [100,101] C;
 pot+water heat capacity >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water at 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when salt at room temperature Tr dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- room / initial water temperature, C -/
noncomputable def Tr0 : ℝ := 20
/-- admissible range of the boiling point of the salt solution, C -/
noncomputable def TbLo : ℝ := 100
noncomputable def TbHi : ℝ := 101
/-- minimum heat capacity of pot + water, J/K -/
noncomputable def CwMin : ℝ := 4170
/-- salt/water mass-ratio regime -/
noncomputable def rLo : ℝ := 1/50
noncomputable def rHi : ℝ := 1/20
/-- NaCl molar mass g/mol, mass of 1 L of water at 20 C in kg -/
noncomputable def Mnacl : ℝ := 58.44
noncomputable def massWater : ℝ := 0.998
/-- moles of NaCl for salt/water mass ratio r (1 kg = 1000 g) -/
noncomputable def nSalt (r : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000*massWater*r/Mnacl
/-- empirical bounds of the secant-mean apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl(aq) over 20 C..Tb, J/K/mol -/
noncomputable def PhiLo : ℝ := -41
noncomputable def PhiHi : ℝ := -27
/-- derived floor for Cs, J/K (n <= 0.854 mol times -41 J/K/mol) -/
noncomputable def CsFloor : ℝ := -36
/-- lower bound of the enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at room temperature, J/mol -/
noncomputable def hsolMin : ℝ := 3800

lemma nSalt_pos (r : ℝ) (h : rLo ≤ r) : 0 < nSalt r := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl; unfold rLo at h; positivity

lemma nSalt_le (r : ℝ) (h : r ≤ rHi) : nSalt r ≤ 0.8540 := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl
  unfold rHi at h
  rw [div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (nSalt r * Phi < 0 ∧ CsFloor ≤ nSalt r * Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold CsFloor
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr0 = T0 dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [rLo,rHi], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [TbLo,TbHi], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0 = Tr0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta <= Tb.
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol : ℝ),
    TbLo ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ TbHi → CwMin ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr0) < P →
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → hsolMin ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, Tr0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Tr0
        ≤ boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  generalize nSalt r = n at hn0 hn1 ⊢
  unfold TbLo at hb1; unfold TbHi at hb2; unfold CwMin at hCw
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold hsolMin at hs
  have hTr : Tr0 = 20 := rfl
  rw [hTr] at hP h1 ⊢
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - 20 := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - 20 ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-20)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - 20 := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h 20 20 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    20 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]

The search found the paper that matches your query. The results don't show the equation itself.

**The paper:** Marc Laliberté, "A Model for Calculating the Heat Capacity of Aqueous Solutions, with Updated Density and Viscosity Data," *J. Chem. Eng. Data* 54(6), 1725–1760 (2009), DOI 10.1021/je8008123. The heat-capacity model is in this paper. The viscosity model you named is a different Laliberté paper, from 2007 (*J. Chem. Eng. Data* 52, 321).

**What the model does:**
- It calculates the heat capacity of aqueous solutions with any number of solutes at any temperature.
- Parameters for 79 solutes were fitted to about 6600 single-solute data points.
- The average difference from experiment is -0.0003 kJ·kg⁻¹·K⁻¹, with a standard deviation of 0.010.
- It was validated on 13 multi-solute systems with 485 data points.

**How the solute term is used:**
- The solution heat capacity is a mass-fraction-weighted sum of the water heat capacity and each solute's apparent heat capacity. SysCAD's documentation describes this as the water specific heat and the solutes' heat capacities combined with a standard mixture Cp calculation.
- A deicer paper applies it to NaCl using the form Cp_solution = w_water·Cp_water + w_solute·Cp_solute.
- In the `thermo` Python library, `Laliberte_heat_capacity_mix` takes the parameters a1 to a6 for each solute. It calculates the heat capacity of an aqueous electrolyte mixture using the form proposed by Laliberté. That function's source is a good place to check the exact form of the Cp,i expression (a1…a6, α) that you quoted.

The pages I retrieved don't state the formula or the a1–a6 values, so I can't confirm your expression from them.

Useful links:
- Library docs: https://thermo.readthedocs.io/thermo.electrochem.html
- SysCAD page: https://help.syscad.net/Species_Table_-_Specific_Heat_(Cp)

Code lines:

- "Cp_i = a1*exp(alpha) + a5*(1. - w_w)**a6"

The code returns the result multiplied by 1000, while the docstring says units are J/kg/K.

**Laliberte_heat_capacity_mix** (docstring equation):

- "Cp_m = w_w Cp_w + \sum w_i Cp_i"

Code lines:

- "Cp_w = Laliberte_heat_capacity_w(T)"
- "w_w = 1.0 - sum(ws)"
- "Cp = w_w*Cp_w"
- "Cp += ws[i]*Cp_i"

**Combination:** The solution heat capacity is a weight-fraction-weighted sum. Water's heat capacity is computed by `Laliberte_heat_capacity_w(T)` and multiplied by the water weight fraction, w_w = 1 − Σws. Each solute's partial heat capacity Cp_i is multiplied by its weight fraction w_i and added to that term.

The search didn't turn up Parker's tabulated Φ_L values for NaCl at 0.5 and 1.0 mol/kg, so I can't give you the molality-dependent numbers.

- **Infinite-dilution value:** The standard enthalpy of solution of NaCl at 25 °C is about +3.88 kJ/mol, and a Purdue reference table gives the same figure. A 2020s-era paper on 303–313 K data (Cited-by section) reports 3.32, 2.78 and 2.25 kJ/mol at 303.15, 308.15 and 313.15 K.
- **Parker's tabulation:** A ScienceDirect paper confirms that Parker tabulated the relative apparent molar enthalpy as a function of molality. Its abstract says its results were compared with values computed using the enthalpy of solution at infinite dilution and the relative apparent molar enthalpy tabulated by Parker.
- **Evaluated NaCl data:** NIST/JPCRD reprints (srd.nist.gov/jpcrdreprint/1.555709.pdf and 1.555660.pdf) say that below 100 °C they rely on the apparent molal enthalpies evaluated by Parker (at 25 °C). They also say the molar heat of solution is related to the apparent molal enthalpy Φ_L.
- **Relationship to use:** The integral enthalpy of solution at molality m is ΔH_soln(m) = ΔH°_soln(∞) + Φ_L(m). So at 0.5 and 1.0 mol/kg you would take 3.88 kJ/mol and add Parker's Φ_L at that molality.

For the actual Φ_L values, check the Parker NSRDS-NBS 2 table directly, or the Pitzer-type model in the JPCRD NaCl papers.

The search supports ΔH_soln° of NaCl(cr) at infinite dilution and 25 °C being about +3.88 kJ/mol. It did not turn up the Wagman 1982 NBS tables themselves, and none of the results gave the exact formation values you listed.

- **Enthalpy of solution:** A Purdue reference table (the CRC-style "Enthalpy of Solution of Electrolytes") gives molar enthalpies of solution at infinite dilution, in kJ/mol at 25 °C. Its entry for NaCl(c) is 3.88. AUS-e-TUTE also lists NaCl(s) at +3.88 kJ/mol. LibreTexts notes that NaCl dissolves endothermically. That page's rounded value is +3.9 kJ/mol.
- **Formation enthalpies of the aqueous ions (Engineering Toolbox):** Cl⁻(aq) is −167.1 kJ/mol and Na⁺(aq) is −240.2 kJ/mol. These agree with your −167.159 and −240.12 to within rounding.
- **Formation enthalpy of the solid (JANAF):** The JANAF table gives ΔfH° of NaCl(cr) at 298.15 K as −411.120 kJ/mol.

Combining the ion values you gave with that solid value gives a consistency check. Using −240.12 + (−167.159) − (−411.12) gives about +3.84 kJ/mol. That is close to 3.88, and the small gap probably comes from the solid's formation enthalpy differing slightly between sources (NBS versus JANAF).

It lists NaCl(c) at +3.88 kJ/mol for 1 mol of crystalline solute dissolved in an infinite amount of water at 25 °C. It cites Parker, NSRDS-NBS 2 (1965).
- **Why that doesn't match the model:** it is a 25 °C infinite-dilution value, whereas my model dissolves salt at 20 °C to about 0.3–0.9 mol/kg. The earlier "NBS ≈ 3.88 kJ/mol at 20 °C" was therefore not matched to the model.
- **Laliberté heat-capacity formula:** from the `thermo` library source, the correlation is Cp_i = a1·exp(a2·t + a3·e^(0.01·t) + a4·w) + a5·w^a6. Here t is in °C, w is the solute mass fraction, and Cp_i is in kJ/kg/K. For NaCl the six coefficients (a1–a6) come from the `chemicals` data table, and its fit range is 1.5–120 °C and w ≤ 0.26. I did not fetch the original paper (J. Chem. Eng. Data 54, 1725, 2009).
- **A simplification:** by definition the salt's heat-capacity contribution is Cs(T) = m_salt·Cp_i(T, w), so no molar mass is needed. As a spot check, Cp_i ≈ −1.05 kJ/kg/K at 20 °C and w = 0.035, which is negative.

It also asks for the temperature coefficient near 20–25 °C. I won't guess this value.

**Plan once the response arrives**
1. **Replace the secant average:** model the solution enthalpy K(T) = Cw(T−Tr) + E(T), with E(T) = n·hsol + ∫nΦ(T) dT and Φ(T) taken pointwise from the Laliberté correlation. Prove that the "salt cools the water" condition holds at every temperature up to Tb, using Φ ≤ 0 so that E is decreasing.
2. **Generalise the timing theorem:** prove "earlier salting is no slower" for this temperature-dependent heat capacity, parametrised by the post-mixing solution temperature so no inverse function is needed.
3. **Prove the bounds in Lean:** show the Φ bounds from the correlation with explicit enclosures for exp (a product (1 ± x/k)^k, split into temperature subintervals). Use Kirchhoff's law to get hsol at 20 °C from the 25 °C value, so only the sign of the salt's solid heat capacity is needed.

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) :
    ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), HasDerivAt (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H)
      (1/(P - h*(H/Cw)) - 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))) H := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  intro H hH
  have a1 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*(H/Cw)) (-(h/Cw)) H := by
    have := ((hasDerivAt_id H).div_const Cw).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have a2 : HasDerivAt (fun H : ℝ => P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (-(h/(Cw+Cs))) H := by
    have := (((hasDerivAt_id H).sub_const D).div_const (Cw+Cs)).const_mul h
    simpa [div_eq_mul_inv, mul_comm] using (this.const_sub P)
  have l1 := (a1.log (hw H hH).ne').const_mul (-(Cw/h))
  have l2 := (a2.log (hs H hH).ne').const_mul (-((Cw+Cs)/h))
  have := l1.sub l2
  convert this using 1
  · funext x; rfl
  · have e1 := (hw H hH).ne'
    have e2 := (hs H hH).ne'
    have e3 := hh.ne'
    have e4 := hCw.ne'
    have e5 := hC.ne'
    field_simp

/-- Salt added at water temperature x changes the temperature at once by
 (D + Cs (x-Tr))/(Cw+Cs) downward (a cooling if D + Cs (x-Tr) > 0).  -/
lemma mix_iff (Cw Cs D Tr x : ℝ) (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) :
    (Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw ↔ 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr) := by
  rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', div_le_iff₀ hC]
  constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

lemma G_mono (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, (H-D)/(Cw+Cs) ≤ H/Cw) :
    MonotoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonneg.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hw H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

lemma G_anti (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw) (hC : 0 < Cw + Cs) (hh : 0 < h)
    (hP : h*(Tb-Tr) < P)
    (hs : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))
    (S : Set ℝ) (hS : S ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr))) (hconv : Convex ℝ S)
    (sgn : ∀ H ∈ S, H/Cw ≤ (H-D)/(Cw+Cs)) :
    AntitoneOn (fun H => Fw Cw P h H - Fs Cw Cs D P h H) S := by
  have hw : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*(H/Cw) := by
    intro H hH
    have : H/Cw ≤ Tb - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  have hd := G_deriv Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs
  apply antitoneOn_of_deriv_nonpos hconv
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS hH)).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · intro H hH; exact (hd H (hS (interior_subset hH))).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro H hH
    have hH' := interior_subset hH
    rw [(hd H (hS hH')).deriv]
    apply sub_nonpos.mpr
    apply one_div_le_one_div_of_le (hs H (hS hH'))
    have := sgn H hH'
    nlinarith

/-- Comparison lemma (sign-free; proved, used by the answer below).  Salt of fixed amount (Cs, D arbitrary sign, C=Cw+Cs>0).
 If between water temperatures Ta1 ≤ Ta2 salt addition always COOLS (D+Cs(x-Tr) ≥ 0 for x in [Ta1,Ta2]),
 adding at Ta1 is no slower than at Ta2; if it always WARMS (≤ 0), adding at Ta2 is no slower. -/
theorem compare_theorem : ∀ (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb : ℝ),
    0 < Cw → 0 < Cw + Cs → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr) < P → Tr ≤ T0 →
    (∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)), 0 < P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) →
    ∀ Ta1 Ta2 : ℝ, T0 ≤ Ta1 → Ta1 ≤ Ta2 → Ta2 ≤ Tb →
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → 0 ≤ D + Cs*(x-Tr)) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2) ∧
      ((∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → D + Cs*(x-Tr) ≤ 0) →
          boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta2 ≤ boilTime Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta1) := by
  intro Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb hCw hC hh hP hTr hs Ta1 Ta2 h1 h12 h2
  have hmem : ∀ x, Ta1 ≤ x → x ≤ Ta2 → Cw*(x-Tr) ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := fun x a b =>
    ⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩
  have hS : Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)) ⊆ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-Tr)) := by
    intro H hH
    exact ⟨by nlinarith [hH.1], by nlinarith [hH.2]⟩
  have hHx : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc (Cw*(Ta1-Tr)) (Cw*(Ta2-Tr)), ∃ x, Ta1 ≤ x ∧ x ≤ Ta2 ∧ H = Cw*(x-Tr) := by
    intro H hH
    refine ⟨H/Cw + Tr, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
    · have : Ta1 - Tr ≤ H/Cw := by rw [le_div_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.1]
      linarith
    · have : H/Cw ≤ Ta2 - Tr := by rw [div_le_iff₀ hCw]; nlinarith [hH.2]
      linarith
    · field_simp; ring
  constructor
  · intro hx
    have m := G_mono Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      exact (mix_iff Cw Cs D Tr x hCw hC).mpr (hx x a b))
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith
  · intro hx
    have m := G_anti Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb hCw hC hh hP hs _ hS (convex_Icc _ _) (by
      intro H hH
      obtain ⟨x, a, b, rfl⟩ := hHx H hH
      have := hx x a b
      have h3 : ¬ ((Cw*(x-Tr) - D)/(Cw+Cs) < Cw*(x-Tr)/Cw) → True := fun _ => trivial
      rw [mul_div_cancel_left₀ _ hCw.ne', le_div_iff₀ hC]
      nlinarith)
      ⟨le_rfl, by nlinarith⟩ ⟨by nlinarith, le_rfl⟩ (by nlinarith)
    simp only [boilTime]; simp only at m; linarith


/-! ## Salt-specific part (NaCl, 1 L of water).  Inputs are labelled; nothing numerical below is a measurement.

 Choices.  Salt = NaCl.  1 L of water at 20 C has mass 0.998 kg (density 0.9982 kg/L).  The salt/water ratio
 r = (salt mass)/(water mass) (NOT the mass fraction of the final solution; that is w = r/(1+r), proved in
 `cs_theorem`).  Regime: 0.02 <= r <= 0.05, i.e. about 20-50 g NaCl in the litre (2-4.8 wt%).
 Cs is DERIVED, not fitted: by definition of the apparent molar heat capacity Phi of the salt in solution,
 (total heat capacity of solution) = (heat capacity of the water) + n*Phi, so Cs = n*Phi, n = moles of salt.
 Phi is temperature dependent; the lumped model needs one constant Cs, so Phi is the SECANT MEAN of Phi(T,m)
 over 20 C..Tb, which makes the linear mixing enthalpy D + Cs (T-Tr) exact at both 20 C and Tb.
 Empirical inputs (EXPOSED PREMISES, constants below), from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation (fit range
 1.5-120 C, w <= 0.26) evaluated numerically outside Lean (a synthetic calculation from a published correlation)
 and from NBS tables: secant-mean Phi in [-41,-27] J/K/mol for r in [0.02,0.05] (computed -40.7 ... -27.9);
 enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) hsol >= 3800 J/mol (NBS +3.88 kJ/mol); boiling point Tb in [100,101] C;
 pot+water heat capacity >= 4170 J/K (at least the 0.998 kg of water at 4.18 J/g/K).
 D = n*hsol is the enthalpy absorbed when salt at room temperature Tr dissolves at Tr. -/

/-- room / initial water temperature, C -/
noncomputable def Tr0 : ℝ := 20
/-- admissible range of the boiling point of the salt solution, C -/
noncomputable def TbLo : ℝ := 100
noncomputable def TbHi : ℝ := 101
/-- minimum heat capacity of pot + water, J/K -/
noncomputable def CwMin : ℝ := 4170
/-- salt/water mass-ratio regime -/
noncomputable def rLo : ℝ := 1/50
noncomputable def rHi : ℝ := 1/20
/-- NaCl molar mass g/mol, mass of 1 L of water at 20 C in kg -/
noncomputable def Mnacl : ℝ := 58.44
noncomputable def massWater : ℝ := 0.998
/-- moles of NaCl for salt/water mass ratio r (1 kg = 1000 g) -/
noncomputable def nSalt (r : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000*massWater*r/Mnacl
/-- empirical bounds of the secant-mean apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl(aq) over 20 C..Tb, J/K/mol -/
noncomputable def PhiLo : ℝ := -41
noncomputable def PhiHi : ℝ := -27
/-- derived floor for Cs, J/K (n <= 0.854 mol times -41 J/K/mol) -/
noncomputable def CsFloor : ℝ := -36
/-- lower bound of the enthalpy of solution of NaCl(s) at room temperature, J/mol -/
noncomputable def hsolMin : ℝ := 3800

lemma nSalt_pos (r : ℝ) (h : rLo ≤ r) : 0 < nSalt r := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl; unfold rLo at h; positivity

lemma nSalt_le (r : ℝ) (h : r ≤ rHi) : nSalt r ≤ 0.8540 := by
  unfold nSalt massWater Mnacl
  unfold rHi at h
  rw [div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; nlinarith

/-- Cs = n*Phi: sign, size, and the ratio conversion. -/
theorem cs_theorem : ∀ (r Phi ms mwater : ℝ),
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → 0 < ms → 0 < mwater →
    (nSalt r * Phi < 0 ∧ CsFloor ≤ nSalt r * Phi) ∧ ms/(ms+mwater) = (ms/mwater)/(1+ms/mwater) := by
  intro r Phi ms mwater hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hms hmwa
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold CsFloor
  refine ⟨⟨by nlinarith, by nlinarith⟩, ?_⟩
  field_simp
  ring

/-- ANSWER.  Accepted model (lumped heat capacity Cw, constant stove power P, Newton loss h, no evaporation,
 salt at room temperature Tr0 = T0 dissolves instantly, enthalpy conserved).  Under the premises above
 (r in [rLo,rHi], Cs = n*Phi, D = n*hsol, Tb in [TbLo,TbHi], stove stronger than losses at Tb), adding the
 salt right at the start (Ta = T0 = Tr0) is no slower than adding it at any later water temperature Ta <= Tb.
 The condition D + Cs (x-Tr) >= 0 (salt addition cools / does not warm) is CHECKED here, not assumed, and
 the 'stove beats losses' condition for the salt solution is derived from it. -/
theorem answer_theorem : ∀ (Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol : ℝ),
    TbLo ≤ Tb → Tb ≤ TbHi → CwMin ≤ Cw → 0 < h → h*(Tb-Tr0) < P →
    rLo ≤ r → r ≤ rHi → PhiLo ≤ Phi → Phi ≤ PhiHi → hsolMin ≤ hsol →
    ∀ Ta : ℝ, Tr0 ≤ Ta → Ta ≤ Tb →
      boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Tr0
        ≤ boilTime Cw (nSalt r*Phi) (nSalt r*hsol) P h Tr0 Tr0 Tb Ta := by
  intro Cw P h Tb r Phi hsol hb1 hb2 hCw hh hP hr1 hr2 hP1 hP2 hs Ta h1 h2
  have hn0 := nSalt_pos r hr1
  have hn1 := nSalt_le r hr2
  generalize nSalt r = n at hn0 hn1 ⊢
  unfold TbLo at hb1; unfold TbHi at hb2; unfold CwMin at hCw
  unfold PhiLo at hP1; unfold PhiHi at hP2; unfold hsolMin at hs
  have hTr : Tr0 = 20 := rfl
  rw [hTr] at hP h1 ⊢
  have hCs : n*Phi < 0 := by nlinarith
  have hCs2 : -36 ≤ n*Phi := by nlinarith
  have hCw0 : 0 < Cw := by linarith
  have hC : 0 < Cw + n*Phi := by linarith
  have hd : 0 ≤ Tb - 20 := by linarith
  have hd2 : Tb - 20 ≤ 81 := by linarith
  -- enthalpy of solution at the boiling point is still >= 0 (checked)
  have hE0 : 0 ≤ hsol + Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Phi + 41) hd]
  have hE : 0 ≤ n*hsol + n*Phi*(Tb-20) := by nlinarith [mul_nonneg hn0.le hE0]
  have hstove : ∀ H ∈ Set.Icc 0 (Cw*(Tb-20)), 0 < P - h*((H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi)) := by
    intro H hH
    have : (H-n*hsol)/(Cw+n*Phi) ≤ Tb - 20 := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hC]; nlinarith [hH.2]
    nlinarith
  refine (compare_theorem Cw (n*Phi) (n*hsol) P h 20 20 Tb hCw0 hC hh hP (by linarith) hstove
    20 Ta le_rfl h1 h2).1 ?_
  intro x hx1 hx2
  nlinarith [mul_nonneg (by linarith : 0 ≤ Tb - x) (by linarith : 0 ≤ -(n*Phi))]
CYCLE 4 REVISION (response to: choose bounded ratio, derive Cs from an explicit model, prove sign/bounds, check conditions).

regime r in [0.02,0.05] (20-50 g NaCl in the litre, 2-4.8 wt%).

Phi from the Laliberte (2009) NaCl correlation (coefficients from the 'chemicals' package data file; fit range 1.5-120 C, w<=0.26),
evaluated by me in Python (synthetic calculation from a published correlation, not a measurement), water Cp from IAPWS values.
Results (J/K/mol): Phi(20C) ~ -74 (r=.02) ... -52 (r=.05) [lit. infinite dilution ~ -88 at 25 C, consistent]; Phi rises toward ~-21..-31 near 70-80 C and falls to ~-31..-45 at 100 C.
So Cs<0 over the whole 20-100 C, r in regime; |Cs| <= ~45 J/K per kg water (about 1% of 4.2 kJ/K). The earlier cycle's sign change near 13 wt% is outside this regime.
Temperature dependence: model needs constant Cs; I use the secant mean Phi over 20..Tb, which makes the linear mixing enthalpy exact at 20 C and Tb.
Computed secant means: -40.7 (r=.02), -33.9 (.035), -27.9 (.05); bounds used [-41,-27]. The true mixing enthalpy E(x)=n(hsol+int Phi) is decreasing, with minimum at Tb:
E(Tb)/n ~ +630 (r=.02), +1170 (.035), +1650 (.05) J/mol, versus hsol=3880. (At r=0.01 it is only ~+200 and for r<~0.01 it would become negative near Tb: the regime was chosen to exclude this marginal case.)
Result: salt addition is endothermic at all temperatures 20..Tb (it cools), so earlier is better; compare_theorem (cooling over an interval favours the earlier addition) gives: salting at the start is no slower than at any later Ta. Lean checks D+Cs(x-Tr)>=0 from the premises and derives the stove-beats-loss condition for the solution (no longer a premise).
Size of effect: temperature drop on mixing <= ~hsol*n/C ~ 0.3-0.7 K at start; time difference is tiny relative to pot/stove variability. Also boil-over/avoid adding salt to boiling water argue for salting early.
Limits: premises (Phi bounds, hsol, Tb range) are inputs; Laliberte correlation accuracy is a few percent, so margin at r=0.02 (~15% of hsol) is adequate but at r<=0.01 the sign is unresolved. Not proved: quantitative time difference; Phi(T) handled by secant mean; no evaporation or salting-induced boiling-point change in time; no real measurement was run.

The search turned up the equation in the SysCAD documentation. It appears to differ from the form in your query.

**Laliberté (2009), *J. Chem. Eng. Data* 54(6), 1725–1760, DOI 10.1021/je8008123.** SysCAD's documentation gives the model as follows:

- **Mixture heat capacity:** Cp_m = w_w·Cp_w + Σ w_i·Cp_i, where w is mass fraction.
- **Solute heat capacity:** Cp_i = a1·e^α + a5·(1−w_w)^a6.
- **Exponent:** α = a2·T + a3·e^(0.01T) + a4·(1−w_w).
- **Units:** T is in °C, and Cp is in kJ/kg·K.

SysCAD writes the second term with a5 and a6, and the exponent α has three terms (a2, a3, a4). Your query wrote it as a2·(1−w_w)^a3, which is a simplified form. The SysCAD version is probably the correct one, but I only saw it on that page and not in the paper itself.

**Other results:**
- The `thermo` Python library has a `Laliberte_heat_capacity(T, ws, CASRNs)` function, which returns J/kg/K. Its documentation is at thermo.readthedocs.io (thermo.electrochem).
- The paper's abstract says parameters for 79 solutes were established, with about 6600 points included.
- A 2008 arXiv paper on solution mining (2008.11068) uses the same model for NaCl.

I did not find the paper's own equation text or its parameter table. The ACS page or the `thermo` source code would confirm the exact form.

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

/-! Model (see report.txt).  H = enthalpy (J) measured from "everything at room temperature Tr".
 Pure water, heat capacity Cw: temperature Tr + H/Cw.
 Salt solution, total heat capacity Cw+Cs>0 (Cs of EITHER sign; literature: Cs<0), dissolution enthalpy D (at Tr) of either sign:
 temperature Tr + (H-D)/(Cw+Cs).  Salt (at Tr) is mixed in instantly, so H is conserved.
 Heater power P constant, loss h*(T-Tr):  dH/dt = P - h*(T-Tr).
 Separating variables, the time to move from H1 to H2 is Fw/Fs(H2) - Fw/Fs(H1) where
 Fw' = 1/(P - h*(H/Cw)), Fs' = 1/(P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs))) (explicit logarithms below).
 Tb = boiling point of the final solution (fixed by salt amount, not by timing). -/

/-- time-integral of dH/(P - loss) for pure water (antiderivative, h>0) -/
noncomputable def Fw (Cw P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -(Cw/h) * Real.log (P - h*(H/Cw))
/-- same for the salt solution -/
noncomputable def Fs (Cw Cs D P h H : ℝ) : ℝ := -((Cw+Cs)/h) * Real.log (P - h*((H-D)/(Cw+Cs)))

/-- Total time to reach Tb from T0 when the salt is added once the water is at Ta:
 heating water alone from T0 to Ta, then solution from the post-mixing state up to Tb. -/
noncomputable def boilTime (Cw Cs D P h Tr T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (Fw Cw P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)) - Fw Cw P h (Cw*(T0-Tr))) +
  (Fs Cw Cs D P h ((Cw+Cs)*(Tb-Tr)+D) - Fs Cw Cs D P h (Cw*(Ta-Tr)))

lemma G_deriv (Cw Cs D P h Tr Tb : ℝ)
    (hCw : 0 < Cw)

@exp_bound : ∀ {x : ℝ},
  |x| ≤ 1 →
    ∀ {n : ℕ}, 0 < n → |rexp x - ∑ m ∈ Finset.range n, x ^ m / ↑m.factorial| ≤ |x| ^ n * (↑n.succ / (↑n.factorial * ↑n))

def validate_source(source, names):
    """Fixed library trust base; reuse hardened no-metaprogramming body contract."""
    from vcs_lean import strip_comments, validate
    code = strip_comments(source)
    imports = re.findall(r"(?m)^import ([A-Za-z0-9_.]+)\s*$", code)
    if not imports or any(not n.startswith("Mathlib.") for n in imports):
        raise ValueError("explicit imports of pinned Mathlib modules only")
    body = re.sub(r"(?m)^import [A-Za-z0-9_.]+\s*$", "", code)
    validate(body, names)
    if re.search(r"\bconclusion_\w*", body):
        raise ValueError("conclusion_ names are reserved for independent extraction")
    # Extractor requires a colon immediately after the theorem name. Fail clearly.
    for n in names:
        if not re.search(r"(?m)^\s*(?:theorem|lemma)\s+" + re.escape(n) + r"\s*:", body):
            raise ValueError("conclusion binders must be inside the statement after ':'")

n*Ph(T);\n    the salt started as crystal with enthalpy Hcr 20. -/\ndef solEnthalpy (n : ℝ) (Hw Ph Hcr : ℝ → ℝ) (T : ℝ) : ℝ := Hw T + n * (Ph T - Hcr 20)\n\n/-- Physical premises (empirical data and definitions), see the review packet. -/\nstructure Premises (r : ℝ) (Cw Hw Ph Hcr Cpcr : ℝ → ℝ) (hinf phiL : ℝ) : Prop where\n  r_lo : 1/50 ≤ r\n  r_hi : r ≤ 1/20\n  hw_deriv : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc (10:ℝ) Hcr 25 = hinf + phiL\n\nlemma w_range (r : ℝ) (h1 : 1/50 ≤ r) (h2 : r ≤ 1/20) : 1/51 ≤ r/(1+r) ∧ r/(1+r) ≤ 1/21 := by\n  constructor\n  · rw [le_div_iff₀ (by linarith)]; linarith\n  · rw [div_le_iff₀ (by linarith)]; linarith\n\nlemma n_range (r : ℝ) (h1 : 1/50 ≤ r) (h2 : r ≤ 1/20) : 0.34 ≤ nSalt r ∧ nSalt r ≤ 0.86 := by\n  unfold nSalt Mnacl\n  constructor\n  · rw [le_div_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; linarith\n  · rw [div_le_iff₀ (by norm_num)]; linarith\n\ntheorem thermo_facts (r Tb : ℝ) (Cw Hw Ph Hcr Cpcr : ℝ → ℝ) (hinf phiL : ℝ)\n    (hp : Premises r Cw Hw Ph Hcr Cpcr hinf phiL) (hT2 : Tb ≤ 101) :\n    StrictMonoOn Hw (Set.Icc 10 Tb) ∧ StrictMonoOn (solEnthalpy (nSalt r) Hw Ph Hcr) (Set.Icc 10 Tb) ∧\n    (∀ x ∈ Set.Icc (20:ℝ) Tb, Hw x ≤ solEnthalpy (nSalt r) Hw Ph Hcr x) ∧\n For 20-50 g NaCl per kg water and 10-101 C the apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl\n    in solution is negative, in (-105, 0) J/(K mol) up to the floor; dissolving the salt LOWERS the total heat capacity\n    of the pot contents by n*Phi in (-91, 0) J/K (< 2.2 % of the ~4.2 kJ/K of the water). -/\ntheorem cs_sign : ∀ (r T : ℝ), 1/50 ≤ r → r ≤ 1/20 → 10 ≤ T → T ≤ 101 →\n    -105 ≤ PhiC (r/(1+r)) T ∧ PhiC (r/(1+r)) T < 0 ∧\n    -91 < nSalt r * PhiC (r/(1+r)) T ∧ nSalt r * PhiC (r/(1+r)) T < 0 := by\n  intro r T h1 h2 t1 t2\n  obtain ⟨w1, w2⟩ := w_range r h1 h2\n  obtain ⟨n1, n2⟩ := n_range r h1 h2\n  have f := phi_floor _ T w1 t1 t2\n  have g := phi_neg _ T w1 w2 t1 t2\n  refine ⟨f, g, ?_, ?_⟩ <;> nlinarith\n\n/-- CONCLUSION 2 (answer). /-! # Salt timing for 1 L of water at 20 C: when should NaCl be added? (cycle 6)

## REVIEW PACKET
SUPPLIED BY THE USER: 1 L water at 20 C in a pot on a stove; salt will be added; goal = salted water reaches
its boiling point soonest; salt amount, pot, heater, room unspecified.

ACCEPTED EARLIER (kept): lumped Newtonian heating dH/dt = P - h (T - 20), constant stove power P, no evaporation,
salt (crystal at 20 C) dissolves instantly at the moment of addition (total enthalpy conserved).

MODEL CHOICES / ASSUMPTIONS (all appear as `Premises` fields or theorem hypotheses):
 * NaCl; r = salt mass / water mass in [1/50, 1/20] (20-50 g in the 0.998 kg of water of 1 L); mass fraction
   w = r/(1+r) (conversion proved in `w_range`), n = 1000*0.998*r/58.44 mol (`nSalt`, bounds in `n_range`).
 * Pot+water enthalpy Hw(T) has heat capacity Cw(T) >= 4100 J/K on 10..101 C (0.998 kg water >= 4.17 kJ/K; pot adds).
 * Apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl in the final solution = Laliberte (2009) correlation `PhiC`
   (empirical coefficients a1..a6 from Laliberte, J. Chem. Eng. Data 54, 1725; fit range 1.5-120 C, w <= 0.26).
   Ph(T) is the apparent molar enthalpy of NaCl in that solution: dPh/dT = PhiC (definition of Phi_C).
   Solution enthalpy = (water at T) + n*Ph(T)   (definition of apparent molar enthalpy; `solEnthalpy`).
 * Crystal NaCl enthalpy Hcr with Cp_cr in [40,100] J/K/mol on 20..25 C (Archer 1992 Part I, Eqs 4-6: Cp(298.15)=50.16;
   my evaluation of the spline gives 50.04..50.16 on 20..25 C).
 * Parker NSRDS-NBS 2 (1965): Delta H_inf(25 C, NaCl(c) -> infinite dilution) = 928 +- 5 cal/mol (printed p.26);
   Phi_L(25 C, m) (Table X, p.50) is RELATIVE to infinite dilution: Ph(25) - Hcr(25) = Hinf + Phi_L.
   For the regime m = 17.1 r in [0.34, 0.86] mol/kg Table X gives Phi_L = 83, 49, 20, -41 cal/mol at m = .2775,
   .5551, .7401, 1.1101 and dPhi_L/dm^(1/2) < 0 for m >= .185, so Phi_L in [-41, 88]; premise used: [-41, 100] cal. cal = 4.184 J.
 * Boiling temperature Tb of the final solution: 20 <= Tb <= 101 C (1 atm, <= 4.8 wt%: ideal elevation <= ~0.9 K); stove
   beats losses at Tb; Tw, Ts = temperature as function of total enthalpy (inverse of Hw, Hs; existence by IVT,
   `inverse_exists`); tau_w, tau_s = elapsed time as function of enthalpy: d tau/dH = 1/(P - h (T(H) - 20)).

PROVED (not assumed): Phi_C < 0 and >= -105 J/(K mol) for all regime/temperatures (concavity + exp bounds, `cs_sign`);
 trapezoid enclosure Ph(101) - Ph(20) >= -3363 J/mol from 11 Taylor-bounded exp evaluations (`ph_drop`);
 dissolution at 20 C >= 3890 J/mol via Kirchhoff (`thermo_facts`); salt addition at any x in [20,Tb] cools
 (Hs(x) - Hw(x) >= 0), solution enthalpy strictly increasing, mixing at 20 C stays above 10 C; and the timing
 comparison (`timing_core`, `answer_theorem`).
CONCLUSIONS: `cs_sign`, `answer_theorem` (salting at the start is no slower than salting at any later water temperature).
-/


/-! ### calculus helpers -/
lemma dmono (F f : ℝ → ℝ) (a b : ℝ) (hF : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc a b, HasDerivAt F (f T) T)
    (hf : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc a b, 0 ≤ f T) : MonotoneOn F (Set.Icc a b) := by
  apply monotoneOn_of_deriv_nonneg (convex_Icc a b)
  · exact fun T hT => (hF T hT).continuousAt.continuousWithinAt
  · exact fun T hT => (hF T (interior_subset hT)).differentiableAt.differentiableWithinAt
  · intro T hT; rw [(hF T (interior_subset hT)).deriv]; exact hf T (interior_subset hT)

lemma danti (F f : ℝ → ℝ) (a b : ℝ) (hF : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc a b, HasDerivAt F (f T) T)

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Deriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Pow.Real
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv
import Mathlib.Analysis.Calculus.MeanValue

noncomputable def Mnacl : ℝ := 58.44

noncomputable def a1 : ℝ := -0.0693559668993322

noncomputable def a2 : ℝ := -0.0782134167486952

noncomputable def a3 : ℝ := 3.84798479408635

noncomputable def a4 : ℝ := -11.2762109247072

noncomputable def a5 : ℝ := 8.73187698542672

noncomputable def a6 : ℝ := 1.81245930472755

/-- apparent molar heat capacity of NaCl in aqueous solution, J/(K mol): Mnacl * (Laliberté solute heat capacity
    Cp_i in J/(g K)); w = salt mass fraction, T in C -/
noncomputable def PhiC (w T : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  Mnacl * (a1 * Real.exp (a2*T + a3*Real.exp (0.01*T) + a4*w) + a5 * w ^ a6)

/-- Time to reach the boiling point when salt is added while the pure water is at Ta: pure-water branch up to
    total enthalpy Hw Ta, then solution branch from the same enthalpy up to Hs Tb.
    τw, τs = elapsed time as a function of total enthalpy on the two branches. -/
def boilTime (τw τs Hw Hs : ℝ → ℝ) (T0 Tb Ta : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  (τw (Hw Ta) - τw (Hw T0)) + (τs (Hs Tb) - τs (Hw Ta))

/-- thermochemical calorie in J (Parker 1965 reports cal) -/
noncomputable def cal : ℝ := 4.184

/-- moles of NaCl for salt/water mass ratio r, in the 0.998 kg of water of 1 L at 20 C -/
noncomputable def nSalt (r : ℝ) : ℝ := 1000*0.998*r/Mnacl

/-- Enthalpy (J) of pot+solution at T, measured from pot+pure water plus crystalline NaCl at 20 C.
    Definition of the apparent molar enthalpy Ph of NaCl in the final solution: solution = (same water at T) + n*Ph(T);
    the salt started as crystal with enthalpy Hcr 20. -/
def solEnthalpy (n : ℝ) (Hw Ph Hcr : ℝ → ℝ) (T : ℝ) : ℝ := Hw T + n * (Ph T - Hcr 20)

/-- Physical premises (empirical data and definitions), see the review packet. -/
structure Premises (r : ℝ) (Cw Hw Ph Hcr Cpcr : ℝ → ℝ) (hinf phiL : ℝ) : Prop where
  r_lo : 1/50 ≤ r
  r_hi : r ≤ 1/20

  hw_cap : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc (10:ℝ) 101, 4100 ≤ Cw T
  ph_deriv : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc (10:ℝ) 101, HasDerivAt Ph (PhiC (r/(1+r)) T) T
  cr_deriv : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc (20:ℝ) 25, HasDerivAt Hcr (Cpcr T) T
  cr_cap : ∀ T ∈ Set.Icc (20:ℝ) 25, 40 ≤ Cpcr T ∧ Cpcr T ≤ 100
  hinf_b : 923*cal ≤ hinf ∧ hinf ≤ 933*cal
  phiL_b : -41*cal ≤ phiL ∧ phiL ≤ 100*cal
  diss25 : Ph 25 - Hcr 25 = hinf + phiL

def verify_candidate(work, manifest, out):
    """Actually compile, audit axioms, recheck kernel and independently extract."""
    from vcs_lean import ALLOWED_AXIOMS, LISTER, listed
    from vcs_extract import run_lean, main as extract
    out = Path(out)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    result = {"ok": False, "check_complete": False, "reviewable": False, "verdict": "not_proved", "time": stamp()}
    try:
        m, proof, narrative = load_candidate(work, manifest)
        snapshot = out / "submitted.lean"
        snapshot.write_bytes(proof.read_bytes())
        (out / "submitted-report.txt").write_bytes(narrative.read_bytes())
        write_json(out / "submitted-candidate.json", m)
        proof = snapshot
        names = list(m["conclusions"].values())
        source = proof.read_text()
        validate_source(source, names)
        targets = "[" + ", ".join("``" + n for n in names) + "]"
        audited = out / "Checked.lean"
        audited.write_text(source + "\n" + "\n".join(f"#print axioms {n}" for n in names) + "\n" + LISTER.replace("ROOTS", targets))
        status, log = run_lean(audited)
        (out / "lean.log").write_text(log)
        result["lean_exit"] = status
        additions = listed(log)
        seen = set(re.findall(r"^'(.+)' (?:depends on axioms:|does not depend on any axioms)", log, re.M))
        axioms = {n: [a.strip() for a in xs.split(",") if a.strip()]
                  for n, xs in re.findall(r"^'(.+)' depends on axioms: \[([^\]]*)\]", log, re.M)}
        unexpected = {a for xs in axioms.values() for a in xs if a not in ALLOWED_AXIOMS}
        disclosed = {a["name"] for a in additions or [] if a["kind"] == "axiom" and a["used"]}
        gaps = [a for a in additions or [] if a["kind"] == "gap" and a["used"]]
        if status != 0 or additions is None or not set(names) <= seen:
            raise ValueError("Lean, kernel recheck or axiom audit failed; see lean.log")
        if any(a not in disclosed and not (a == "sorryAx" and gaps) for a in unexpected):
            raise ValueError("undisclosed axioms")
        if re.search(r"VAS_UNSTATED \S+ used=true", log) or any("⋯" in a["statement"] for a in additions if a["used"]):
            raise ValueError("incomplete assumption disclosure")
        # click's standalone_mode=False gives real exit errors without terminating host.
        try:
            extract.main(args=[str(proof), *sum((["--theorem", n] for n in names), []), "--out", str(out / "extracted")], standalone_mode=False)
        except SystemExit as exc:
            # Extractor explicitly sys.exit(0), even outside standalone mode.
            if exc.code != 0:
                raise ValueError("independent extraction failed") from exc
        extraction = read_json(out / "extracted.json")
        if not extraction.get("ok") or not extraction.get("compiles_alone") or not all(extraction.get("faithful", {}).values()):
            raise ValueError("independent extraction/statement comparison failed")
        # Any added premise or gap, even unused, remains explicitly conditional.
        result |= {"ok": not additions, "check_complete": True, "reviewable": True, "verdict": "proved_if" if additions else "proved",
                   "added_assumptions": additions, "conditional_on": sorted(unexpected),
                   "extraction": extraction, "scientific_relevance": "requires human review"}
    except (Exception, SystemExit) as exc:
        result |= {"ok": False, "error": str(exc) or type(exc).__name__}
        if isinstance(exc, (subprocess.TimeoutExpired, TimeoutError)):
            result["verdict"] = "no_verdict"
    write_json(out / "verdict.json", result)
    return result


class NativeAuthenticationError(ValueError):
    """Native subscription credentials/status need owner intervention."""


def native_codex():
    executable = shutil.which("codex")
    if not executable:
        raise ValueError("Codex CLI is not installed")
    js = Path(executable).resolve()
    candidates = list(js.parent.parent.glob("node_modules/@openai/codex-linux-x64/vendor/*/bin/codex"))
    candidates += list(js.parent.parent.glob("vendor/*/bin/codex"))
    if not candidates:
        raise ValueError("native Codex binary not found; specify --codex-binary")
    return candidates[0]


def native_claude():
    executable = shutil.which("claude")
    if not executable:
        raise ValueError("native Claude CLI is not installed; specify --claude-binary")
    return Path(executable).resolve()


def client_name(config):
    # States from the original release have no agent field.
    return config.get("agent", "codex")
